#2810. Fed's John Williams indicates higher interest rates likely 'over time' to address inflation

New York Federal Reserve President John Williams said Monday that policymakers have more work to do to bring inflation under control, though he indicated that further action can unfold over time as the economy continues to grow.

In remarks consistent with previous statements, the influential central banker expressed confidence that inflation will get closer to the Fed's 2% target in 2027 before hitting the goal the following year. He also said that while artificial intelligence is pressuring prices, there's "little evidence" it will be the source of more persistent inflation.

"But getting inflation back to 2% is 100% essential," he said in remarks at the University of Mount Saint Vincent in New York. "We must take actions over time that will ensure that we get inflation back to our 2% goal in a timely manner and on a sustained basis. There is still work to do to get that done."

The comments come at a delicate moment for monetary policy, as the economy continues to grow and inflation stays well above the Fed's goal.

Fed officials have differed over how quickly to raise rates, with some advocating more aggressive action while others, including Williams, have emphasized a measured approach.

Williams ascribed the current inflation pressures to the conflict in Iran, demand for goods associated with the AI build-out and tariffs, the effects of which he said "have largely abated" as a source of pressure. Nevertheless, he expressed confidence that inflation will subside substantially in 2027 even as economic growth remains robust and unemployment edges lower.

The Federal Open Market Committee next meets on Dec. 8-9, with markets fully pricing in a quarter percentage point interest rate increase from the current target range of 3.75%-4%. The remarks from Williams are expected to be the last major policy statement from any Fed official before the December decision.

"Monetary policy cannot move ships or reopen pipelines and refineries, but it can — and must — ensure that these shocks do not lead to broader or persistent inflationary dynamics or erode well-anchored inflation expectations," Williams said, according to his prepared remarks.

Williams did not explicitly address the December meeting expectations, instead pointing to the statement released after the prior FOMC meeting in late October that said the "accumulation of more information" will help decide the future policy path.

"The data that we have received since then show that the economy has remained strong and that inflation has continued to run too high," he said.

#2808. Amazon faces FAA probe after delivery drone strikes Michigan fiber cable, garage

The Federal Aviation Administration said Monday it will investigate a recent Amazon drone crash near Detroit.

On Friday, one of Amazon's MK30 drones "landed on a resident's garage" during a delivery in Huntington Woods, Michigan, a suburb north of Detroit, Amazon spokesperson Jack Stelzner told CNBC in a statement.

The crash occurred after the drone dropped off a customer's shampoo order around 4:30 p.m. local time, and one of its propellers was damaged in the incident, according to ABC affiliate WXYZ.

A preliminary incident report posted to the FAA's website states the drone "struck a fiber cable, a residential garage, and became entangled in power lines during takeoff." The report says the condition of the drone is unknown.

Stelzner said there were no injuries, the drone has been removed and Amazon is in contact with the resident.

"Safety is our top priority and incidents are extremely rare," Stelzner said. "Anytime a delivery does not go as planned we investigate what happened and use the findings to further improve the service."

An FAA spokesperson confirmed it's investigating the incident. A spokesperson for the National Transportation Safety Board, a federal agency that also investigates aviation accidents, said it's aware of the incident and is continuing to gather information. The agency hasn't yet determined whether it will open a probe, the NTSB spokesperson added.

Amazon has been working to make widespread drone delivery a reality ever since founder Jeff Bezos laid out his vision for the program nearly 13 years ago. The rollout has been slow, as the program encountered regulatory delays and staff cuts, as well as technical challenges and pushback from some communities where it conducted early pilots.

Expansion has recently ramped up. The company is targeting a launch of its drone service in 500 U.S. cities and towns by the end of the year, while an Amazon executive claimed it has the "highest demand drone delivery service in the industry," CNBC previously reported.

The e-commerce giant has encountered some challenges along the way. Federal investigators opened a probe nearly one year ago after an Amazon drone downed an internet cable in Waco, Texas. One month earlier, a separate federal probe was opened into an incident near Phoenix, where two Amazon drones collided with a construction crane.

#2804. Federal Reserve announces College Fed Challenge winners

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The University of California, Los Angeles won the 23rd Annual National College Fed Challenge.

The University of California, Los Angeles (UCLA) won the 23rd annual national College Fed Challenge on Friday, a team competition that encourages undergraduate students to learn about the U.S. economy, monetary policymaking, and the role of the Federal Reserve System. Teams analyze economic and financial conditions and formulate a monetary policy recommendation, modeling the Federal Open Market Committee. The team, from Los Angeles, California, represented the San Francisco District and included Aarushi Maheshwari, Annika Chan, Atul Prasanth, Caitlyn Cox, Finn Karish, Eli Brooks (alternate), Kennedy Yang (alternate), Manasi Vartak (alternate), Ronojoy Borpujari (alternate), and Sahil Sudani (alternate). The team's advisers were Chris Surro and Ali Haider Ismail.

"I am very happy to congratulate the students who competed in this year's College Fed Challenge, especially the winners and finalists," said Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh. "They took on the same big questions we policymakers face. I'm impressed by their talent, their rigor, and their eagerness to think hard about monetary policy and the economy."

This year's College Fed Challenge included 141 schools from 34 states across the nation, the most in the competition's history. Teams submitted video presentations or participated in local competitions last month. Twenty-one semi-finalist teams then participated in question-and-answer sessions held earlier this month, and seven finalists were invited to the Federal Reserve Board for the national finals. The other national finalists were the University of Chicago in second place and the University of Maryland, College Park in third place. Teams with honorable mentions include Cleveland State University, Harvard College, Pennsylvania State University, and St. Lawrence University.

Teams were evaluated on their economic analysis, responses to the judges' questions, teamwork, and presentations.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

#2799. Congress shreds Nvidia's 'outrageous' decision to bring back Huang: 'Failure of character'

Members of Congress from both parties on Monday expressed outrage and incredulity, and doubled down on their calls for investigations, after Nvidia announced it has reinstated Jensen Huang as its president and CEO.

The resurrection of Huang, who had resigned from the artificial intelligence behemoth just 11 days earlier, raises more questions about the company's decision-making and the Trump administration's national security assessments, on top of existing concerns about Nvidia's advanced AI chips being diverted to China, the lawmakers said.

"It's inexplicable to me why they want this guy back as CEO," Rep. Don Bacon, R-Neb., a China hawk who is retiring from Congress, told CNBC in a phone interview. "To me it's a failure of character."

President Donald Trump told CNBC in an extended interview later Monday morning that he personally intervened to convince Nvidia's board to bring Huang back, saying, "I made the deal."

Bacon, chairman of the House Subcommittee on Cyber, Information Technologies, and Innovation, said he wants the next Congress to conduct thorough oversight, while questioning the Trump administration's motives.

"Somebody's responsible for this, and it may be" Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick, whose department is responsible for setting U.S. export controls on AI chips, Bacon said. "But I think we should get to the bottom of this."

Huang, who is scheduled to testify before multiple committees on Capitol Hill starting Dec. 1, is "going to get high scrutiny in a bipartisan way when he comes next week," Bacon said.

Huang's closed-door appearance before a trio of House committees is on the schedule, as the panels seek answers on the national security implications of Huang's relationship with a suspected Chinese spy, MS NOW reported. The Senate banking committee announced after Huang's reinstatement that he will appear for a public hearing Dec. 3.

John Fisher of Trident, a spokesman for Huang, declined to comment on the Nvidia CEO's scheduled appearances.

"He better be there," Rep. Gabe Amo, D-R.I., a member of the House Foreign Affairs Committee, told CNBC about Huang's scheduled Dec. 1 appearance. His reinstatement will only intensify congressional probes into Nvidia and its government connections, Amo predicted.

"I think it frankly broadens the scope of the query, and it makes Howard Lutnick and his dealings — supporting his nepo children's operation at Cantor Fitzgerald — party to inquiry as well as any other administration official that has facilitated a status quo in which chips can be sold to China or Chinese operated intermediaries who don't intend good things for the American people," said Amo, referring to the financial firm run by Lutnick's sons that has business ties to Nvidia.

A Cantor spokesperson didn't comment directly when asked about Amo's comments, saying the firm "holds no material proprietary position in Nvidia" and noting its Nvidia positions "arise primarily from equity swaps executed for clients."

The Commerce Department didn't immediately respond to requests to comment on Bacon's and Amo's remarks.

Nvidia in a statement Monday said it "received input from senior levels of the United States government" and that it is confident that Huang's reinstatement "poses no national security risk."

David Rudenstine, a constitutional law professor and national security scholar at Cardozo School of Law, said the frenzy around Huang's comeback is secondary to lingering national security concerns."The real responsibility for national security is with the administration and not with the CEO of a private company," Rudenstine told CNBC."You're opposed to China getting the chips. You recognize that the intermediary companies are sending chips to China, and you don't put a stop to it. I don't get it," he said.

The CEO's reinstatement, while celebrated by the White House and in Silicon Valley, led to heightened calls on Capitol Hill to seek answers on AI chip exports to China and what the Trump administration's role has been in facilitating that.

"We are sending high-end computer chips to China, and yet we're saying we want to win the AI war. It's a contradiction, and it's a failure of leadership in the White House," Bacon told CNBC.

Sen. Ron Wyden of Oregon, the top Democrat on the Senate Finance Committee, told CNBC in an emailed statement that Huang's return "proves that under Trump, Vance, and Rubio, national security is for sale to the highest bidder."

"We will all pay the price of letting China plunder America's cutting edge technology. Democrats will be pushing for accountability for anyone in the administration that put corrupt deals over our country's security," Wyden, who is also a Senate Intelligence Committee member, said.

Trump in a Truth Social post Monday added that Attorney General Todd Blanche "has confirmed that the U.S. Government does not possess evidence that he has engaged in any unlawful conduct."

Lawmakers, who have already launched multiple investigations related to Huang, Nvidia and related entities, quickly filed in to question those conclusions.

Sen. Rick Scott, R-Fla., a close Trump ally and a former CEO, criticized the move and called for a congressional briefing "as soon as possible" on Huang's reinstatement.

"Nvidia's board is free to vote however they want, but the federal government owes the American people answers about what actually happened," Scott told CNBC in an emailed statement. "This looks terrible on its face, and it drives home the need for Congress to be informed on the full impact of Jensen Huang's actions on our national security and economy."

Rep. Scott Peters, D-Calif., a member of the House Energy and Commerce Committee, told CNBC that it's still not clear to what degree Huang's relationship with a suspected Chinese spy might have compromised national security.

"The fact that the president of the United States is involved in putting him back in there, presumably because maybe he has stock or he just cares about the stock market, it's really reached a bizarre place," Peters said. "And obviously that man has not learned anything from the election about what people think of him, or he doesn't care."

Monday's move also gave fuel to lawmakers who have already questioned Trump, his administration or Nvidia.

"The reckless decision to bring Jensen Huang back as CEO does not change the fact that Mr. Huang, NVIDIA executives, and the company's board still owe Congress answers about the company's compliance with U.S. export controls," Sen. Elizabeth Warren, D-Mass., said in a statement via the Senate Banking Committee, where she is the top Democrat and is expected to become chair in January.

Sen. John Cornyn, R-Texas, more tersely questioned the official assurances that Huang poses no risks to national security.

"No national security risk? Based on what?" he wrote on X.

Rep. Raja Krishnamoorthi, D-Ill., who serves on the House Select Committee on the Chinese Communist Party, in an X post called Nvidia's decision to reinstate Huang "outrageous."

"We still don't know what information Chinese intelligence may have obtained from him, and neither the government nor Nvidia's own board has completed its investigation," wrote Krishnamoorthi, who is also a member of the House Oversight and Intelligence panels.

— CNBC's Dan Mangan, Garrett Downs and Luke Fountain contributed to this report.

#2793. Lakers rookie discovers surprising NBA perks he never dreamed of

There are several commonly known perks of being an NBA player.

Chartered flights. State-of-the-art facilities. And a lot of free, high-quality food are among them.

Cameron Carr #43 of the Los Angeles Lakers smiles during a game against the Utah Jazz. NBAE via Getty Images

But there are several that aren't as well known that an athlete doesn't become aware of until they're in the NBA.

Consider Cameron Carr among the players learning about the benefits of his profession.

The Lakers rookie wing was posed with the question of unexpected benefits of being an NBA player that he didn't anticipate after Monday's shootaround ahead of his team's home game against the Jazz at Crypto.com Arena.

Cameron Carr of the Los Angeles Lakers drives to the basket for a layup. NBAE via Getty Images

"I'll probably say NBA League Pass," Carr responded. "Got NBA League Pass for free. Never had NBA League Pass."

The former Baylor Bear couldn't contain his excitement over getting the NBA's product for free that can cost $159.99/year or $24.99/month.

Now, he can watch any NBA game at any time.

"So s— great," Carr said before covering his mouth in a slight panic for cursing. "It's great, by the way."

Cameron Carr of the Los Angeles Lakers speaks to the media after a game. NBAE via Getty Images

Sandro Mamukelashvili, a six-year veteran, understood Carr's excitement.

"Yeah, it's amazing," Mamukelashvili said. "Watch every game [for] free. Give it to your parents. I definitely enjoy my free league pass."

The second perk appealed to Carr's appreciation of pregame naps.

"They do like the Sleep Number stuff for the rookies," he said. "They do like a discount code, so that's great."

He added: "I'm a big sleep guy. Like as soon as I get done with this, I'm gonna shower, go home, and take a nap."

#2791. How the rest of Jets season could shake out — and what it may mean draft and Aaron Glen

That was the message the Jets delivered to their fans with Sunday's 27-24 victory over the Chargers. A segment of Jets fans now view wins as losses because it damages the team's draft position, but Jets players and coaches are trying to win games, not worry about next April's draft.

The Jets now are 3-8 with six games remaining. Some of those games look winnable, so the pro-tank crowd now is bracing itself for more sad Sunday nights watching their draft positioning sink.

It starts this week against a Dolphins team that is starting Kyle McCord at quarterback. The Jets play the Cardinals on Dec. 20, when Arizona could be starting rookie Carson Beck or 40-year-old journeyman Josh Johnson under center. The next week, they face a suddenly vulnerable Patriots team that may have ex-Giants backup Tommy DeVito as its starter. The Jets finish the season in Buffalo; the Bills, who are currently 9-1, may have their playoff position wrapped up by then and could play backups.

All of this has Jets fans worried about a potential 6-11 or 7-10 finish that could send their draft pick outside of the top 10.

The Jets have three first-round picks in 2027, so even if the Jets win some games down the stretch, they could end up with a high pick. Indianapolis traded the Jets its first-round pick last year in the package for Sauce Gardner, and the Colts' pick looks destined to be in the top five. At the time, the Colts seemed playoff-bound, but they have bottomed out and now are playing out the string.

The Colts fired general manager Chris Ballard, traded away some of their key players and just released starting quarterback Daniel Jones. At 3-8, it's hard to see the Colts winning many more games with Anthony Richardson Sr. now starting at quarterback surrounded by very little talent.

Will McDonald IV #9, Jowon Briggs #91 and Minkah Fitzpatrick #29 of the New York Jets celebrate after a stop against the Los Angeles Chargers. Getty Images

The Colts face the Giants, Eagles, Titans, Bengals, Browns and Jaguars to finish the season. They might beat the one-win Titans, but it is hard to see them beating anyone else — even the Eagles.

I used ESPN's playoff predictor to simulate the rest of the season, allowing analytics predict the outcome of every game except for those of the Colts and the Jets. I had the Colts going 1-5, with the win coming over the Titans, to finish 4-13. I gave the Jets wins over the Dolphins, Cardinals and Patriots to finish 6-11. Using the ESPN simulation and team's current strength of schedule, the Colts pick would be No. 4 and the Jets' own pick would be No. 11. The Jets also have a pick from the Cowboys, which will be the better of either that of Dallas or Green Bay. That pick figures to be in the middle of the first round.

This scenario would give the Jets one top-five pick, but they most likely would be able to package that pick and their own to move up if they wanted to jump up to take a targeted quarterback. Their main issue is going to be the Dolphins likely picking ahead of them. In my simulation, Miami did not win another game and finished with the top overall selection. They likely would take the top quarterback in that scenario and would not be interested in trading it to the Jets.

The other fear that many Jets fans have about the team winning more games centers around head coach Aaron Glenn. Many fans have seen enough from him and hope owner Woody Johnson will fire him after the season. Glenn is 6-22 since the start of his tenure last year, and he has had many game management issues.

There is a fear among some Jets fans that Johnson will elect to retain Glenn if he goes 6-11 or 7-10. No one knows what Johnson currently is thinking, but he likely is to take into account who the Jets beat and not just the number of wins. In other words, Johnson would see that victories over bottom feeders like the Chargers, Dolphins and Cardinals do not mean the Jets have turned the corner under Glenn. He likely would need to pull off an upset or two over the good teams remaining on the Jets schedule — the Broncos, Vikings or Bills, if they are playing their starters.

#2787. Asian Nvidia-related stocks jump after Huang's return, with Taiwan's near price limits

Asian technology stocks jumped on Tuesday as investors piled back into the sector, following the reinstatement of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang by its board.

Key Nvidia partners and suppliers in Asia saw their shares skyrocket. Taiwan's TSMC, a critical partner to the U.S. chip giant, surged 9.85%, with Hon Hai Precision Industry — known also as Foxconn — also up by 9.95%. Nvidia partner Mediatek also spiked 9.91%. Taipei's stock exchange has a daily price limit of 10% for stocks.

In South Korea, Nvidia suppliers Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix jumped 6.7% each.The two companies, which together make up over 40% of the Kospi's weighting, sent the index up over 6%. South Korea's LG Electronics soared 15.86%.

The rise in the index triggered the Korea Exchange's sidecar on the index early in the day, reportedly making it the 100th sidecar the KRX has activated. Japan's Softbank Group Corp, a major investor in the chip space, climbed over 8% before paring gains to 4.66%.

Semiconductor test company Advantest, as well as counterpart Tokyo Electron, jumped 12% and 7%, respectively. Both companies are collaborators with Nvidia.

The exuberance did not follow into European markets at the open, with the region's technology sector down 0.3% in early deals, weighing on a broader 0.14% rise in the benchmark Stoxx 600 index. Europe's tech index popped more than 5% on Monday.

"Jensen's return is particularly welcome for Asian technology stocks, many of which suffered collateral damage from Nvidia's leadership crisis," Charu Chanana, Chief Investment Strategist at Saxo said in a note Tuesday morning.

The recovery will likely favor established companies with visible orders, strong balance sheets and pricing power, compared to those who rely on "years of uninterrupted AI spending," Chanana added.

Nvidia's stock had closed up 31% following Huang's reinstatement. It had previously shed nearly 33% from the time Huang admitted to an extramarital affair on Nov. 8.

#2781. Spurs roll past Kings despite Victor Wembanyama missing first game of the season, De'Aaron Fox sidelined

The San Antonio Spurs were without both Victor Wembanyama and De'Aaron Fox on Monday night.

That didn't stop them from rolling past the Sacramento Kings.

Wembanyama was ruled out of the Spurs' 123-107 win over the Kings due to back tightness, the team announced shortly before the game was set to tip off at the Golden 1 Center. Fox is dealing with a shin contusion, and was ruled out at the same time.

While losing two star players for any contest is undoubtedly concerning, the two injuries were described as simply "bumps and bruises" by San Antonio Express-News' Jeff McDonald. Spurs coach Mitch Johnson said both players are not expected to be sidelined long, but would not yet declare they would be ready to return for their NBA Cup matchup against the Portland Trail Blazers on Wednesday.

The game against the Kings is the first that Wembanyama has missed all season. He's averaged 29.9 points, 12.3 rebounds and 3.9 blocks through the first 17 games of the season, all of which are career highs for the 22-year-old. He's fresh off a wild 50-point outing on Saturday night. Wembanyama outscored the New Orleans Pelicans starters by himself in the first half before rolling to a dominant win. It was his second 50-point game already this season, following a 52-point performance against the Dallas Mavericks earlier this fall.

Fox has averaged 12.4 points and 5.8 assists per game this season. The 28-year-old is in his second full season with the Spurs, who landed him in a deal with the Kings during the 2024-25 campaign. Johnson said Fox was injured when he was intentionally tripped by a Pelicans player.

And though the Kings jumped up early in the opening frame, the Spurs rallied and led the entire second half to secure the 16-point win. Stephon Castle led the way with a near-triple-double. He had 28 points, 11 assists and eight rebounds on the night. Julian Champagnie added 22 points, and Luke Kornet finished with 18 points and 11 rebounds.

Domantas Sabonis led the Kings with 20 points, 12 rebounds and eight assists in the loss, which dropped them to 2-15 on the season. Zach Lavine added 18 points, and De'Andre Hunter finished with 15 points and eight assists.

The Spurs now hold a 15-3 record, which is tied for the best in the league with the Los Angeles Lakers. Though both Wembanyama and Fox should be back on the floor very soon, the team is clearly capable of holding things down without them when necessary.

#2772. Pass the gravy — Rams turn Packers game into a Thanksgiving feast

SoFi Stadium will be home for the NFL's first-ever Thanksgiving Eve game to take place between the Rams and Packers on Wednesday night, and they're doing everything they can to keep the holiday spirit in the game.

According to Arash Markazi, the founder of The Sporting Tribune, the Rams will have a special Thanksgiving menu that will be sold to fans at SoFi Stadium and available to purchase three hours before kickoff.

The menu will include smoked turkey legs served with cranberry barbecue sauce, a turkey pot pie, and smoked turkey with mashed potatoes that includes gravy with herb dressing and cranberry sauce.

According to The Sporting Tribune, the fans will have a few different options to eat turkey on bread, as SoFi Stadium will be selling a smoked turkey burnt ends sandwich, as well as panes con pavo, pairing turkey with crisp vegetables and the tang of curtido.

Los Angeles Rams mascot Rampage handing out turkeys at SoFi Stadium last week. AFP via Getty Images

For desserts, fans will have a few options available as miniature pumpkin, pecan, and Dutch apple pies will be sold, and hot apple cider will be on the menu.

The Rams will be selling a few drink options in the stadium, which include the "Ramsgiving Ponche," a blend of guava, apple, hibiscus, tamarind, and warm spices. Fans will also be able to purchase it with tequila or as a mocktail.

Rampage is handing out turkeys to football fans ahead of Thanksgiving. AFP via Getty Images

According to Markazi's post, fans will be able to purchase a special souvenir sipper that is an imitation of a miniature Rams water cooler.

The Rams' Thanksgiving menu is one of the many highlights that will take place in Inglewood, as the rock and roll band U2 will be performing live at halftime.

Rams quarterback Matthew Stafford posing for a photo on the Los Angeles sideline. AP Photo/Caroline Brehman

Los Angeles will also be debuting their new "Fearsome White" uniforms to honor the legendary Fearsome Foursome defensive line that dominated the NFL in the 1960s.

The Rams and Packers will kick the game off at 5:00 p.m. PT and will stream live on Netflix.

#2769. Blazers shoot the lights out as Embiid-less Sixers fall to 14-4

We haven't seen them yet this year — do all Western Conference teams just never miss their three-pointers?

The Sixers were blown out by the Trail Blazers 128-111 Monday as Portland made 45% of their threes on the night.

After missing his last two games, Jaylen Brown was soaring until he ran into foul trouble, finishing with 20 points on 8-of-14 shooting along with two rebounds and four assists. LeBron James had his best shooting night as a Sixer, going 8-of-13 from the floor to finish with a season-high 23 points along with seven rebounds and three assists.

Tyrese Maxey had 22 points and five assists on 7-of-16 shooting. VJ Edgecombe finished with 12 points, seven rebounds and seven assists shooting 5-of-11 from the field. Deni Avdija led all scorers with 31.

Joel Embiid (illness) was the only Sixer out tonight. Ja Morant and Shaedon Sharp were out for Portland, both out with long-term injuries.

The Sixers got Brown involved with two early touches. He drew a foul on the first and got an open jumper on the second thanks to a healthy stiff arm to Avdija. Jabari Walker found Edgecombe by the basket as the Blazers were trying to get down the floor, capping off a 9-2 Philly run to start the game and prompt an early timeout for the Blazers.

That timeout got Avdija going offensively. After beating Brown off the dribble he nailed a couple of threes. LeBron was creating threes on the other end as well. A pumpfake of his sent Avdija flying by, allowing James to drill an open one from the corner. He drove baseline on the next possession and found Brown, also wide open in the corner.

Coming off the bench, Dominick Barlow was the first able to stop Avdija, swatting away a layup attempt. The Sixers didn't come out with a poor effort defensively. They were able to lock up Damian Lillard for a sho-tclock violation, but Portland opened the game hot from deep, making four of their first nine threes of the game.

Adem Bona also returned after missing time with a neck injury. He was able to quickly grab an offensive rebound upon entering the game and when the ball got swung to him, he was able to put it on the deck and get to the basket for a dunk. He even hit a bit of a move to get by Jrue Holiday. He was relieved by Walker after only a couple of minutes. Walker picked up a missed Dean Wade three and put it back to keep the Sixers up by two after the first.

With some more starters back on the floor the offense started to hum again. Brown and Edgecombe each drilled pull-up jumpers. LeBron threw a pair of nice passes to Walker who converted on both of them, and got a skyhook for himsel.

Defensively, Walker was a pretty small look against Donovan Clingan. The Sixers tried to sit in some zone to combat that, but quickly went back to man. On top of those paint concerns, the Blazers remained pretty hot from three. They hit two more to take a lead of their own, despite LeBron hitting a big three that got the crowd off it's feet in between them.

Brown did not look like he had missed the last two games at all. He just looked so smooth getting to his spots in the midrange. He put up 19 in the first half on 10 shots. The Sixers needed every bit of it because they had less answers for Avdija on the other end.

After starting the game with Brown on Avdija, the Sixers tried Dean Wade before ultimately landing on Barlow to close the half. Barlow did foul him but Avdija missed both free throws. On the next trip down Barlow was able to force a bad airball that happened to fall to LeBron who threw a gorgeous fullcourt pass to Brown for the fast break dunk. After that tied the game, Lillard was able to draw a foul on Edgecombe shooting a three-pointer. Maxey found LeBron in the corner on the ensuing possession for another Sixers' buzzer-beater, keeping the game tied at the half.

LeBron tried to throw another fullcourt bounce pass to give Maxey a fast break layup, but Sidy Cissoko got his hands on it to break it up. They continued to move the ball well though as Edgecombe got open in the dunker for James to find him, before dishing to a cutting Walker who drew a foul. Both teams struggled from the line to start the second half as Walker split his pair. Avdija was unable to convert an and-1 and Clingan gave the fans a five-count of chicken nuggets very early.

The Sixers also had to deal with another extended period of Brown on the bench after he picked up his fourth foul. James seemed to still be up to carry the scoring load, drilling his fourth three of the night on his fourth attempt.

For as good as LeBron was, the Sixers struggled to supplement him offensively with Brown sitting in foul trouble. They had a six-minute stretch only scoring one field goal. Maxey tried to get more aggressive but had a pull-up three attempt blocked by Robert Williams III. Lucky for him the Blazers weren't able to secure the rebound and Maxey was able to scoot to the basket for a reverse layup.

Any minor blip for the Sixers offense was notable because they just could not get stops on the other end. As they sat in a zone Portland continued to drill everything from deep. After Scoot Henderson drifted into a gap to drill a three, Holiday hit one in Wade's face on the following possession. On the other end Barlow was desperately trying to create a hustle bucket, but he came up a bit gingerly after going for a rebound and getting his shot blocked by Cissoko. Jayson Kent drilled one from the corner to give the Blazers a nine-point lead as the third came to a close.

Henderson and Kent drilled a couple more threes to start the quarter, extending Portland's lead to its largest of the night. Nick Nurse tried to let it ride as LeBron got to the basket, but after Edgecombe dribbled the ball off his foot out of bounds and the team surrendered a layup off an offensive rebound, Nurse called an angry timeout less than two minutes into the quarter.

Edgecombe was able to run out off a loose ball and get a transition layup and the Blazers immediately called a timeout to squash a potential run. The Sixers had chances coming out of the timeout, forcing a couple turnovers, but they couldn't take advantage on the offensive end. Lillard hit a pull-up which got the Blazers offense rolling again and the Sixers had no response.

Portland's height advantage had begun to wear the Sixers down as well as they started surrendering more. offensive rebounds. No matter what center option the Sixers tried, Portland's center would have an inch or two in height advantage, there was just nothing to be done about that, save for some Tacko time. Avdija stole an inbounds pass of off Maxey as Sixers fans started to flock to the exits with over five minutes remaining.

#2768. Nvidia has lost $2 trillion in value. Here's what options traders expect from earnings

Jensen Huang's forced exit from Nvidia shocked Wall Street and Washington, wiped $2 trillion off its market value, and raised serious questions about national security and regulation of the AI complex. Traders, for the moment, must content themselves with the comparatively mundane business of Tuesday's earnings, and options traders are expecting big moves.

With volatility in the stock near the highest since April 2025, options traders are now pricing a 7% reaction in the stock after its report, the biggest implied move going into an Nvidia report since February 2025, and well above the median move of 3.2% over the last eight quarters.

Implied volatility reached 68 Monday, compared to under 40 in early October, according to ThinkOrSwim data. Nvidia vol reached a multi-year high above 92 in the aftermath of President Trump's tariff Liberation day in April '25. In other words, the company that for much of the AI boom was the most reliable winner in the market, is now more volatile than all but 35 companies in the S&P 500.

"Longer term, I see significant downside risk unless Nvidia can attract an exceptional leader, someone like Tim Cook or AMD's Lisa Su," Nigam Arora, founder of the Arora report, said in a text ahead of the company's after-market report on Tuesday.

Since hitting all-time highs in October, Nvidia shares have fallen 35%, shedding $2 trillion in market capitalization along the way. Including Nvidia, there are only five companies in the S&P 500 worth more than $2 trillion. However, there is evidence that bulls have been holding out hope while bears snatch up profits: the ratio of open puts to call contracts has actually widened towards calls, according to Barchart data. The ratio is currently 0.87, down from a one-year high of 1.02 two weeks ago and the lowest since the first week of October.

The most popular contract by volume on Monday was the 155-strike put expiring at the bell Monday, but the most popular trade for later expiry was the 160-strike call expiring Thursday, and the second-most-common purchase was the 190-strike call expiring Friday, SpotGamma data show.

One catch is that most call contracts traded Monday looked like neutral to bearish positions opened by traders selling calls. Of the 5 million options traded on Monday, 3 million were calls, but more were likely opened by sellers than buyers. Of the $2.3 billion in options premium traded on Nvidia Monday, $1.2 billion was tied to puts, SpotGamma data show.

With bears taking profits in puts the past month, there's another potential snag on open interest. There are very few open put contracts at or below the $157 level at which the stock closed Monday. That suggests any bad surprise would find many traders with little downside protection.

By the same token, there's notable open interest in call contracts from the 175-strike and above, suggesting profit-taking on a bounce could limit Nvidia's recovery past levels it breached over the past week.

Either way, Tuesday's earnings could provide fireworks.

#2766. Mike McCarthy: Aaron Rodgers is our quarterback

Before an NFL team benches its starting quarterback, someone asks the coach whether the quarterback could be benched.

That happened for the first time during Aaron Rodgers's two-year tenure with the team on Monday, during a press conference with coach Mike McCarthy, one day after the team's latest loss, to the Eagles.

It came up as innocuously as possible. McCarthy wasn't asked whether Rodgers could get the hook; McCarthy was asked whether he's considering using one of the other three quarterbacks on the roster in an effort to mix things up when the offense is struggling.

"Aaron Rodgers is our quarterback," McCarthy said.

Of course he is. Even if Rodgers's play merits benching, Aaron Rodgers isn't getting benched. It's implicit in the arrangement.

Still, Aaron Rodgers is a far cry from what he once was. In 10 games this season, Rodgers has completed 220 of 361 passes for 2,241 yards, with 16 touchdown passes and six interceptions. His passer rating of 86.6 is the lowest of his career since becoming Green Bay's starter in 2008, with the exception of a four-snap performance in 2023 with the Jets (39.6, based on one incomplete pass thrown).

At 6-4, the Steelers remain in contention for another one-and-out playoff appearance. That makes it easier to justify staying the course. With upcoming games against the Broncos, Texans, and Jaguars, that could change.

Even then, Rodgers would never be benched. He'd be "injured." And his career would end not with a bang but with a whimper.

#2764. Trump administration proposes rule that could curb ESG investing in 401(k)s

The Trump administration proposed a rule on Monday that officials said they expect to severely cripple so-called ESG investing in workplace retirement plans such as 401(k)s.

The proposed regulation, if finalized, would amend provisions under the Employee Retirement Income Security Act of 1974, or ERISA, to clarify how employers and other plan "fiduciaries" can apply their duty of loyalty to 401(k) investors.

The rule affirms that the people who choose investments for their company retirement plan must make decisions "solely in the financial interests of workers and retirees, not to advance political or social agendas," U.S. Secretary of Labor Keith Sonderling said Monday in a written statement.

ESG — short for environmental, social and governance — funds let people invest according to certain values such as curbing climate change or improving corporate diversity. They're also known as socially responsible, sustainable, impact or values-based funds.

"We make clear in this rule that when you are selecting investments for a retirement plan ... you may not have any ulterior motives or collateral interests," a Labor Department official said during a press briefing Monday morning.

"We believe that will inform all fiduciaries as to whether an impact fund or ESG fund is appropriate for retirement funds," the official said. "We believe the answer will be very rarely."

Already, few 401(k) plans offer ESG funds — partly due to whipsawing federal policy around their use.

About 5% of 401(k) plans offered an ESG fund in 2025, according to a Plan Sponsor Council of America report issued earlier this month. On average, less than 1% of overall investor assets in 401(k) plans are held in ESG funds, the report said.

The Labor Department proposal, which is subject to a 60-day public comment period, comes as the Trump administration has tried to make it easier for employers to add alternative investments such as private equity funds to workplace retirement plans.

It also comes amid a broader Trump administration backlash against ESG and policies it views as "woke."

Within days of his inauguration in 2025, President Donald Trump announced the U.S. would withdraw from the Paris Agreement, blocked subsidies for electric vehicles, pushed for more fossil-fuel production, and started a "huge pushback" against diversity, equity and inclusion policies, Diana Iovanel, a senior markets economist at Capital Economics, wrote in a research note last year.

"Our country will be woke no longer," Trump said during a speech to a joint session of Congress in March 2025.

More recently, Trump and the Republican-led Congress repealed key elements of the Inflation Reduction Act, former President Joe Biden's signature law to combat climate change. In April, a White House report said ESG investing has a "significant cost to investors and the broader economy."

Monday's proposal undoes elements of a Biden administration rule in 2022, Labor Department officials said. That Biden-era regulation sought to loosen ESG investing rules put in place in 2020 by the first Trump administration. The Biden rule, for example, clarified that employers could weigh the economic effects of climate change and other ESG considerations when making investment choices, officials said at the time.

Among the ways the latest proposal seeks to curb ESG uptake in 401(k) plans is by eliminating the "tiebreaker test" and the "participant preference provision," the Labor Department said.

The tiebreaker test says, for example, that if an ESG fund is "tied" with another fund after comparing various financial factors, a 401(k) plan sponsor can choose the ESG fund because of its social benefits, according to legal experts.

Current rules also allow employers to select funds based partly on their workers' preferences, provided they still meet certain financial criteria.

Data shows that ESG funds may provide stronger financial performance than that of more traditional funds, however.

For example, sustainable funds returned investors a median 4.9% in the first half of 2026, versus 4% for traditional funds, according to a Morgan Stanley analysis in September.

"Sustainable funds also continue to outperform over the long term: A hypothetical $100 investment into a sustainable fund in December 2018 — the start date for Morningstar's sustainable fund classification — would have grown to $171 today, while investing $100 into a traditional fund over the same period of time would have become $159 today, assuming median returns were achieved in each period," according to the report.

#2763. It’s time for Basketball: San Antonio Spurs vs Sacramento Kings

Welcome to the Game Thread. Veterans of the Game Thread know how we do things around here, but for all you newbies we have a few rules. Our community guidelines apply and basically say be cool, no personal attacks, don't troll and don't swear too much.

The Spurs didn't play particularly well on Saturday night, despite blowing out the Pelicans 114-91. You could say it's the hallmark of a good team that they can win big when they're not playing great, but in truth, it was all Victor Wembanyama, who dominated the Zion-less shorebirds all by himself with a 50 point masterclass, along with 18 rebounds, 5 blocks, 4 steals and d0minating just about every stat that you can read in a box score. Well, the big guy is questionable tonight, and with Dylan Harper still rehabbing his sore hip and Fox questionable with a shin contusion, this is a chance for Castle to lead the team to a win, and for Champagnie and Vassell to make some major contributions. Also, Jordan McLaughlin is listed as questionable, so we could see some Ja'Kobi Gillespie minutes, who has only played a little over 4 minutes in the NBA so far, but has been tearing up the G-League with the Austin Spurs.

The opponent tonight is the Sacramento Kings, who only have two wins so far in the season. One of those wins was just a few days ago, as they beat the Celtics 97-96 in the Golden 1 Center. The Celtics rested several of their players, including Jason Tatum, but it shows that in the NBA, good teams can't take wins for granted just because they're playing a team that has a bad record, especially if you're missing key players. The Spurs will have to play with poise and keep their focus to come out with a win tonight, and a fast start is key. If the Silver and Black can take the Kings out of the game early, they could keep the home crown from giving the Kings enthusiasm and come out with a comfortable win. If the Kings hang with the Spurs into the fourth quarter, it could end up with a purple laser beam piercing the sky as the home team lights the beam for the third time this season.

GO SPURS GO!! It's another late night game, which is going to be on the menu all week, as the Spurs face the Portland Trail Blazers on Wednesday night at 9:00 in the Moda Center, and return to the Frost Bank Center to face the Lakers on Friday night with an inexplicably late 8:30 PM start time. Both games are NBA Cup games, and if San Antonio wins both, they will win their group outright. #2760. ESPN continues Tom Jackson tributes during 'Monday Night Football'

The tributes for Tom Jackson continued as ESPN aired the Nov. 23 edition of "Monday Night Football" between the Cincinnati Bengals and Washington Commanders.

As play-by-play announcer Joe Buck opened the broadcast, the "NFL Primetime" music played in the background. Jackson, the former ESPN NFL studio analyst who co-hosted the weekly Sunday highlight show alongside Chris Berman for nearly three decades, died Friday at the age of 75. No cause of death has been provided.

"He was a wonderful being first and foremost," ESPN color commentator Troy Aikman said during the pregame show.

As a player, Aikman said, Jackson and Berman were must-see television – both in an entertainment sense but also because that was how fans saw what happened around the league that week.

The former Dallas Cowboys quarterback respected that they could be critical and opinionated without attacking the individual.

"I could throw three interceptions in a game, and we could lose, and I could watch Primetime and not wanna go hang myself at the end of the night," Aikman said. "They just had a respectful way of presenting the information and letting everybody know what happened, but doing it the right way, I thought."

During the beginning of the second quarter, Buck welcomed viewers over the "Primetime" theme once again.

"There are a lot of heavy hearts in our company as the NFL and ESPN family has lost a legend this past week," Buck said.

Aikman repeated his stance that "Primetime" was "must-watch" every Sunday.

"He was obviously a big part of the football season for many years," Aikman said.

Berman announced Jackson's death Nov. 22 during "Sunday NFL Countdown." To honor the former Denver Broncos linebacker who was part of the vaunted "Orange Crush" defense, the ESPN crew sported orange ties throughout the show.

ESPN also released a five-minute video of Jackson surprising Berman at the studio just a few weeks before his unexpected passing.

During halftime of Monday night's game, Berman hosted a special edition of his "Fastest 3 Minutes" segment solo. After once again eulogizing his best friend on air, Berman did the highlights of the Broncos' 38-24 victory over the Las Vegas Raiders on Sunday. Then in typical "Boomer" and "Teej" fashion, the package shifted to highlights of Denver's 1977 AFC championship game victory, including postgame locker-room footage of Jackson, over the Raiders.

A special "TJ" logo with his Jackson's jersey number, 57, appeared in the upper right hand corner of the screen throughout halftime.

#2758. Observations after Sixers' 5-game win streak ends with blowout loss to Blazers

Observations after Sixers' 5-game win streak ends with blowout loss to Blazers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers' five-game winning streak was snapped Monday night by the Trail Blazers.

With a 128-111 defeat at Xfinity Mobile Arena, the Sixers dropped their first game of the year against Western Conference opposition and fell to 14-4. Portland improved to 11-6.

LeBron James had a season-high 23 points and six rebounds. Tyrese Maxey scored 22 points and Jaylen Brown added 20.

Deni Avdija led the Blazers with 31 points and seven assists. Donovan Clingan posted 15 points and 18 rebounds.

The Sixers were missing Joel Embiid (illness). Ja Morant (left hamstring strain) and Shaedon Sharpe (right knee meniscus surgery) were among Portland's sidelined players.

Next on the Sixers' schedule is a Wednesday night game vs. the Heat. Here are observations on their blowout loss to the Blazers:

Jabari Walker, listed at 6-foot-7, defied conventional wisdom by winning the opening tip-off against the 7-2 Cllingan. Maxey then canned a three-pointer.

Brown scored the Sixers' next four points with a pair of free throws and a short jumper. VJ Edgecombe's layup gave the team a 9-2 lead. Brown and Dean Wade both returned Monday after sitting out the last two games with left hamstring injuries.

Avdija got hot in the first quarter. He scored 11 quick points, made his first four field goals and helped the Blazers avoid a serious early deficit.

The Sixers took a 32-30 edge with 9.3 seconds left in the first when Walker jammed in a put-back dunk. The former Portland second-round draft pick played 17 minutes in his second start of the season and continued to look deserving of a rotation spot. He posted 10 points and five rebounds.

Adem Bona was available again following an eight-game absence with a cervical spine sprain. However, Dominick Barlow checked in first to back up Walker.

Barlow had an outstanding start to his night. He scored a clever lefty push shot over Clingan and threw down a dunk in the first quarter. Barlow also made a highlight defensive play in his opening stint, defending Avdija in isolation and rejecting his layup attempt.

Bona then subbed in for Barlow with 3:23 left in the first quarter. He had a positive first possession, grabbing an offensive rebound and driving in for a dunk.

Wade later played at center, too. Barlow logged 16 minutes and had six points. Bona recorded four points and two boards in five minutes. Sixers head coach Nick Nurse ended up using a 10-man rotation and did not play rookie guard Labaron Philon Jr. until fourth-quarter garbage time.

Brown delivered tons of efficient offense for the Sixers in the first half and sure didn't appear rusty. His layup late in the second quarter tied the game at 58-all. The five-time All-Star forward scored 19 first-half points on 8-for-10 shooting.

Avdija exceeded Brown's production, hitting heat-check threes, leaners through contact and just about everything he put up regardless of the defender. He tallied 25 points in the first half and shot 9 for 12 from the field.

James reached 16 points when he capped a high-scoring half by nailing a corner three. The contest was even at 69 apiece heading into halftime.

Monday's game was James' first of the season with three or more made three-pointers. The four-time MVP went 4 for 5 beyond the arc. He'd gone 10 for 46 (21.7 percent) over his first 13 games and was due for a good long-range night.

James was whistled for his third foul early in the third quarter. Brown picked up his fourth with 10:15 to go in the period, forcing him to sit for an extended stretch. Wade entered and James remained aggressive on his way to a third straight 20-point outing.

Avdija stopped lighting up the Sixers in the third period, but the team still had another unimpressive defensive quarter. Portland posted 32 points in the third and built its lead to double figures in the opening minute of the fourth.

The game devolved into a blowout. An Avdija dunk put the Blazers' advantage at 22 points with 5:15 on the clock and the Sixers didn't have a miracle comeback in store.

Even with all their wins, there's no question the Sixers have been trending downward on defense over the past couple of weeks, allowing regular dribble penetration and plenty of comfortable threes.

They'll aim to raise their level on that side of the floor and expect Embiid's presence to be a major help once he's back in action.

#2755. Six key questions raised by Jensen Huang's return to Nvidia

Jensen Huang made a dramatic return as Nvidia CEO on Monday, less than a week after board Chairman Harvey Jones said directors did not intend to reinstate him. The board later voted unanimously to bring Huang back.

Nvidia shares surged as much as 36% during the session and closed 31% higher.

The reversal followed a steep decline in Nvidia shares, which closed at $125.53 on Friday even after the company reported strong third-quarter results. A petition calling for Huang's return had drawn more than 200,000 signatures, according to its organizers.

The rally spread across the broader market, with the Nasdaq Composite gaining 6.5% and the S&P 500 rising 4.7%, each posting its strongest session since April 2025.

Nvidia's board said it had received input from senior U.S. government officials and that its investigation into Huang's conduct would continue.

Still, the board's reversal, President Donald Trump's administration's role and continued scrutiny of Nvidia's China business leave major questions for both investors and lawmakers.

Sen. Jack Reed, a member of the Senate Banking Committee, urged Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent and Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick on Monday to appear before the committee to discuss the case.

Reed did not lay out a specific list of questions for the two officials, but his statement cited reporting that Trump had been briefed by intelligence officials led by then-National Security Adviser Marco Rubio before intervening in support of Huang's return.

Reed's call adds to mounting pressure on both Nvidia and the Trump administration to explain what they knew and when.

Rep. Gabe Amo, D-R.I., vice ranking member of the House Foreign Affairs Committee, said Huang’s reinstatement could broaden congressional scrutiny rather than bring it to a close.

“I think it frankly broadens the scope of the query,” Amo told CNBC, adding that lawmakers should also examine administration officials involved in decisions affecting Nvidia and chip sales to China.

Amo said he expects the planned closed-door interview with Huang on Dec. 1 to proceed and that Congress would continue seeking answers both privately and publicly in the coming months.

“He should start purchasing his plane tickets to Washington,” he said. “It is abundantly clear that this is ripe for questioning by Congress.”

On Nvidia's third-quarter earnings call on Nov. 17, Jones said the board had accepted Huang's resignation and did not intend to reinstate him.

Less than a week later, the board reversed course, saying Monday that it had unanimously voted to bring Huang back after discussions with him and input from senior U.S. government officials.

Trump said he had spoken with board members and that his administration provided assurances based on the information available to it. But the board has not publicly detailed what it was told.

"The board decided to do what we wanted to do," Trump told CNBC, adding that he believed his intervention had been important.

"I've been speaking to the board directly and indirectly, and I felt very strongly about it," he said.

Trump also called into an Nvidia board meeting to argue for Huang's return, Semafor reported, citing people familiar with the matter.

Reed has called on Huang to disclose his communications with Trump and on Nvidia to release a transcript of the board's call with the president.

Lawmakers are still seeking answers about the alleged smuggling of Nvidia products to China, their possible diversion for military use and possible advancement of Chinese AI capabilities.

The Senate Banking Committee has scheduled Huang to testify on U.S. export controls on Dec. 3. House lawmakers have also confirmed closed-door interviews with Huang on Dec. 1 and Nvidia General Counsel Tim Teter on Dec. 2.

"The Nvidia board's brief internal investigation and reinstatement of Jensen Huang do not erase the national security threats and questions that must be addressed," Reps. John Moolenaar, Rick Crawford and Brian Mast said in a joint statement.

Their inquiry may also examine what Nvidia knew about the customers and resellers involved, as well as what controls the company had in place.

Other lawmakers have also sought to preserve the paper trail. Sens. Elizabeth Warren and Chris Coons had asked senior administration officials on Nov. 11 to retain records related to Nvidia exports of advanced AI chips to China and associated national security concerns.

Nvidia has promised to hire a chief compliance officer and establish a committee overseeing national security and export controls. The board's investigation into Huang's conduct also remains underway.

Josh Gilbert, eToro's lead analyst for APAC and the Middle East, said investors will be watching whether those new safeguards carry meaningful authority.

Gil Luria, head of technology research at D.A. Davidson, said Nvidia has a limited window to prepare for the possibility that a future president may be less supportive of Huang.

With Trump's term ending in two years, Luria argued that Nvidia needs a clearer plan for who could take over if Huang were forced to leave again.

Gilbert pointed to Nvidia's roughly 75% revenue-growth forecast for the next financial year as evidence of strong near-term demand. The longer-term question, he said, is whether customer orders and artificial intelligence investment can sustain that pace.

Luria told CNBC that concerns about the broader AI trade remain, including the cost of financing data centers, potential overbuilding by neocloud companies and obstacles to building new facilities.

Bernstein analyst Stacy Rasgon said worries about whether AI spending is achievable and sustainable were not caused solely by Huang's departure. UBS, meanwhile, flagged Nvidia's roughly $390 billion in supply commitments and the need to keep demand flowing through its ecosystem.

Gilbert said part of Monday's rally reflected investors recovering earlier losses rather than pricing in new growth. Bernstein restored its "Outperform" rating and kept its $200 price target, while UBS maintained its $220 target.

"For the share price, it comes down to whether Nvidia can keep delivering as it has done over recent years," said Gilbert, adding that Huang's return could ease some concerns about Nvidia's longer-term outlook.

"The real worry has been what happens beyond next year. Having Huang back at the helm goes a long way to easing those concerns, because he's the one who sits down with the biggest customers and gets those multi-year orders over the line," he said.

— CNBC's David Sucherman, Justin Papp and Eko Prasetyo contributed to the story

#2750. Barlow looks set for season debut with Sixers after long days, ‘miserable' weeks

Barlow looks set for season debut with Sixers after long days, 'miserable' weeks originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Dominick Barlow is on the verge of his season debut with the Sixers.

The team listed Barlow as probable for its Saturday afternoon matchup with the Bucks at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

Barlow practiced Friday and has been making clear progress in his recent on-court work recovering from an injury formally classified as a "lower back stress reaction."

He was a reliable rotation fixture last season, playing 71 regular-season games and starting 59. Barlow posted 7.7 points, 4.8 rebounds, 1.2 assists and 0.9 steals per contest in 23.8 minutes per game. The Sixers then made a common-sense call to exercise his $3.4 million option for the 2026-27 season.

The 23-year-old described his back injury as "an overuse thing."

"I guess there was an area or maybe I started my (offseason) workouts too early," he said. "I don't know what it was fully in terms of what caused it. Once it happened, it happened. Nothing I can do about it besides try to correct it and get my body feeling as good as possible."

He called the past few weeks "miserable" as he rehabbed and waited on the green light by the Sixers' training staff.

"The days just felt longer," Barlow said. "Trying to be engaged mentally, which I think I've done a good job with, but the physical aspect wasn't there. I'm somebody that's prided himself on competing, being available. Not being able to do that was hard, but I'm excited to kind of put this past me."

With LeBron James and Jaylen Brown on the team now, Barlow won't start at forward when the Sixers are at full strength.

He's still a multi-dimensional frontcourt piece for Sixers head coach Nick Nurse who may very well get substantial opportunities at backup center.

"Just cutting, playing hard, being ready to make the open shot," Barlow said. "And then I don't think you can ever have too much defensive versatility. I think that's something I can bring, try to help us be more versatile."

When Barlow checks into his first game of the season, he'll appreciate the moment.

"You don't really realize what you have until it's not there (anymore)," he said. "I'm just excited to play. …. Just playing the first stint … that's going to be the main thing I'm looking forward to."

Dean Wade (left hamstring soreness/tightness)

#2749. AI adoption grows in Asia, but measuring the gains remains tricky

Businesses across Asia-Pacific say their investments in artificial intelligence are paying off, with the technology helping boost productivity and revenue. But putting a number on those gains is proving far less straightforward.

Some 58% of businesses using AI said their investments had already surpassed their initial costs, yet just 12% were very confident in their ability to accurately measure their return on investment, according to research conducted by Strand Partners for Amazon Web Services.

The survey included 9,750 business leaders across 10 Asia-Pacific markets: Australia, Indonesia, Japan, Malaysia, New Zealand, the Philippines, Singapore, South Korea, Thailand and Vietnam.

The biggest shift over the past year has been the growing number of companies moving AI projects into production, Jaime Vallés, AWS vice president for Asia Pacific, Japan and China, told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

"From year over year, probably the biggest difference is you're seeing a lot of ideas put into production," Vallés said.

Despite the reported financial benefits, many businesses remain in the early stages of AI adoption.

The survey found that 64% of businesses using AI across Asia-Pacific and Japan still rely on general-purpose AI tools or ready-made solutions for specific tasks, while only 19% of businesses have a formal and comprehensive AI strategy.

Among those with such an AI strategy, 35% had reached the most advanced stage of adoption, compared with just 8% of businesses without one, according to the research.

Technology alone is not enough to turn AI investments into meaningful business outcomes, Vallés said. He pointed to what he called the "four Rs of readiness": technological, cultural, skills and personal readiness.

"Where we see more meaningful impact is when you see the leadership using AI, building a culture of using AI and using AI to transform and not automate," Vallés said.

Financial services is among the sectors seeing stronger AI adoption. Vallés pointed to Vietnam's Techcombank as an example, saying the bank is using AI to assess loan applications and expand access to credit for small businesses.

The technology has helped the bank to analyze more data and approve some loan applications that might otherwise have been rejected, he said.

Techcombank, one of Vietnam's largest commercial banks, serves more than 16.5 million retail and corporate customers. According to an AWS case study, its modernization of digital banking infrastructure using AWS improved application response times by up to 35% and cut the time needed to launch new product features by 30%.

AI adoption continued to expand across Asia-Pacific, with all 10 markets surveyed recording increases from the previous year.

Australia led the group with an adoption rate of 61%, followed by Singapore at 59% and South Korea at 58%.

Still, businesses face significant obstacles to scaling AI investments. A shortage of AI and digital skills remains a key barrier, cited by 48% of respondents as an obstacle to adoption or expansion.

Despite those challenges, Vallés expressed optimism about Asia-Pacific's role in AI innovation, pointing to the region's cultural diversity and range of perspectives.

"I am convinced that most of the innovation in the fullness of time will come from our region," he said.

Vallés added that Asia-Pacific has the ingredients to become a global center for innovation, citing its diversity, talent, startup ecosystem and technology.

"APAC, to make it simpler, has all the ingredients to be in my view the core or the center of worldwide innovation," he said.

Governments across Asia also play a role in supporting Al adoption and addressing barriers to its wider use.

About 72% of government respondents said Al had increased productivity, according to a report released by the Pacific Economic Cooperation Council in November, which surveyed more than 500 experts across 23 Asia-Pacific economies.

Around 59% of respondents viewed government support as a moderately or very important factor in accelerating AI adoption next year, though existing regulations were also seen as a constraint, the report showed.

Such support is also taking share through regional cooperation. The Asian Development Bank and South Korea established a $20 million fund to help developing economies adopt Al and address gaps in digital skills and infrastructure, according to an announcement by the bank earlier this month.

#2748. The Fearsome Foursome set the standard. Can the Rams’ new front four live up to it?

The Los Angeles Rams are bringing back the Fearsome Foursome on Wednesday night.

Not the one that terrorized NFL quarterbacks in the 1960s. That group belongs in football immortality.

This one has Aaron Donald, Myles Garrett, Kobie Turner, and Byron Young. Four defensive linemen with enough talent to make opposing quarterbacks think twice about hanging on to the football.

They haven't all been healthy enough to wreak havoc together.

Byron Young (left), Kobie Turner (left-middle), Braden Fiske (right-middle) and Myles Garrett (right) wearing the Rams' inaugural "Fearsome White" jerseys that will be unveiled during the Thanksgiving Eve game between the Green Bay Packers and Los Angeles Rams on Netflix (Credit: Los Angeles Rams)

With Turner returning from a groin injury and Garrett finally finding his footing after knee surgery, the Rams could have their entire defensive front available for the first time this season when they host the Green Bay Packers on Thanksgiving Eve.

And the timing couldn't be more appropriate.

The Rams will unveil their Fearsome White alternate uniforms Wednesday, paying tribute to the legendary defensive line of Deacon Jones, Merlin Olsen, Rosey Grier and Lamar Lundy.

Grier will be honored on the field alongside family members of the original Fearsome Foursome, while the Rams will wear throwback-inspired uniforms celebrating the franchise's defensive dominance of the 1960s.

But the current group doesn't want to spend the evening playing dress-up.

They want to make some history of their own.

"When we go out there and play to the best of our ability, and we rush four as one, five as one, we're a very, very scary unit," Turner said Monday. "I don't want to put too much out there up front. I want our work to be able to show itself, and I think it's going to show itself on Wednesday."

The Los Angeles Rams defensive front four, Lamar Lundy, left, Merlin Olsen, Rosey Grier, and Deacon Jones, pose in a 1964 photo. AP

That's a fairly bold statement from a player who hasn't shared the field with the Rams' complete collection of pass rushers this season.

Donald is a three-time NFL Defensive Player of the Year. Garrett is the reigning champ and has won the award twice. Young has developed into a dangerous edge rusher, while Turner provides the kind of interior disruption that can collapse a pocket before a quarterback finishes his drop.

Before the season, this group looked like a dream for defensive coordinator Chris Shula.

But on the field, it has largely remained an unfinished experiment.

Los Angeles Rams defensive tackle Aaron Donald (99) greets defensive end Myles Garrett (95) during an NFL game in Landover, Md., Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. AP Photo/Stephanie Scarbrough

Garrett understands the history he's being asked to honor.

The Rams linebacker revealed Monday that he studied Jones while growing up, watching footage of the Hall of Fame pass rusher who helped popularize the quarterback sack as an official measure of defensive dominance.

"I was watching Deacon Jones in high school, watching 'Top 100,' listening to him speak, him coining the term 'sack,' how much he's done for this game," Garrett said.

But Garrett's admiration apparently went beyond watching old highlights.

Asked whether he ever tried Jones' infamous head slap, a technique that was eventually outlawed by the NFL, Garrett couldn't help laughing.

"In high school I did, for sure," Garrett admitted. "I got away with it for a little while too."

Somewhere, Jones would probably appreciate that confession.

Donald, meanwhile, said he has spent his Rams career trying to uphold the standard established by the franchise's original defensive giants.

"Coming here as a rookie, you heard about it," Donald said. "I always try to live up to their success and everything they were able to do."

The challenge Wednesday isn't simply honoring history. It's stopping a Green Bay offense capable of turning a broken play into a 50-yard touchdown.

Packers quarterback Jordan Love has developed a dangerous connection with his deep threats, including Christian Watson and Matthew Golden.

But throwing deep requires time, and that's precisely what the Rams hope to take away.

"They're going to take their shots. They're going to test our back end," Turner said. "But for them to even have that opportunity, they have to hold the ball a little bit longer. And that's where this defensive front comes into play."

Braden Fiske, Kobie Turner and Byron Young of the Los Angeles Rams celebrate a sack during a game against the New Orleans Saints in Inglewood, Calif., Nov. 2, 2025. Getty Images

Defensive coordinator Chris Shula has resisted comparisons between his current group and the original Fearsome Foursome, preferring to emphasize chemistry, preparation and getting his best players healthy.

It should be pointed out that the original four earned their reputation over years of dominance, not one nationally televised game.

But there's something fitting about the Rams finally unleashing their complete defensive front on a night dedicated to the men who helped redefine the position.

"It's our job to go out there on Wednesday night in front of the entire world and show that legacy, and make sure that they're proud," Turner said.

The Rams will provide the white uniforms, the vintage graphics and the historical tribute.

The rest is up to Donald, Garrett and company. After all, the best way to honor the Fearsome Foursome isn't by looking like them. It's by making Jordan Love feel what opposing quarterbacks felt 60 years ago.

#2746. Malick Thiaw: The Squad Are “100% Behind Head Coach Matthias Jaissle”

Malick Thiaw has delivered a strong message of support for Newcastle United head coach Matthias Jaissle, insisting that the entire squad remain "100 per cent" behind the German manager despite the growing noise and scrutiny surrounding the club's recent performances.

Questions have inevitably been raised about Newcastle's direction following a difficult spell of results, with supporters understandably demanding greater consistency and improved performances from a side that has ambitions of competing at the highest level.

However, Thiaw has made it clear that there is no division within the dressing room and that the players remain fully committed to Jaissle, his methods and the standards he expects from his squad.

Speaking when asked whether the Newcastle players were 100 per cent behind their head coach, the defender gave an emphatic response.

"Yes, 100 per cent. There's always noise in football, especially outside of the locker room, but we know what we have to do and what the coach expects from us."

It was a straightforward but important statement from the Newcastle defender, particularly at a time when speculation and outside criticism can quickly become distractions for a team struggling to find consistency.

Thiaw's comments suggest that, internally, there is no question over the players' relationship with their manager. Instead, the focus remains on translating that support into performances on the pitch.

Importantly, the defender was also keen to acknowledge that the responsibility does not rest solely with the head coach.

"We are also on the pitch and have to deliver and do better."

That admission is perhaps the most significant part of his response.

While managers naturally attract the majority of attention when results do not go according to plan, players must also accept responsibility for their performances, decision-making and execution during matches.

Jaissle arrived at Newcastle with a clear footballing philosophy, built around aggressive pressing, vertical football, intensity and developing a team capable of competing against the strongest sides in the Premier League.

However, implementing those principles takes time, particularly in a demanding league where opponents are quick to punish mistakes and weaknesses.

For Newcastle, the challenge is ensuring that the manager's ideas become second nature rather than something the players struggle to execute consistently.

Thiaw's comments indicate that the squad understand what is being asked of them. The next step is demonstrating that understanding when it matters most.

There is also an important distinction between supporting a manager and blindly defending performances. Newcastle supporters want to see evidence of progress, improved organisation and a team that consistently matches the intensity expected of them.

The players can publicly back their head coach, but ultimately, results will determine how much confidence and belief surrounds this project.

For Jaissle, having the dressing room behind him is an important foundation. A fractured relationship between players and manager would only make an already challenging situation more difficult.

Thiaw's words should therefore provide some reassurance that the German retains the backing of those inside the training ground.

Nevertheless, Newcastle cannot afford to rely on statements of unity alone. The squad must now take responsibility, respond to the criticism and show that they are capable of delivering the football Jaissle wants to implement.

The message from Thiaw is clear: the players are 100 per cent behind Matthias Jaissle. Now they must prove it on the pitch.

For Newcastle supporters, that is ultimately what matters most.

#2745. Christian McCaffrey puts 49ers’ loss of ‘true warrior’ in perspective

Christian McCaffrey had the closest view of anyone on the 49ers roster when Colton McKivitz suffered a gruesome injury that ended his season during Sunday night's 35-10 victory against the Vikings in Mexico City.

On Monday, McCaffrey posted a photo of himself consoling the 49ers' right tackle on his Instagram story, sharing his gratitude.

49ers Colton McKivitz seen carted off the field in Sunday's game against the Vikings. AFP via Getty Images

"A true warrior. A staple of this team. Love you brother

@cmckivitz53," McCaffrey captioned the photo of him hugging McKivitz after the injury occurred.

McKivitz was seen landing on top of McCaffrey in an awkward position on the play that derailed his season.

On Monday, the 49ers announced that he suffered a fractured and dislocated left ankle on the play.

San Francisco 49ers running back Christian McCaffrey rushing down the field during Sunday's game against the Vikings. Getty Images

McCaffrey was one of the many 49ers players who were seen left devastated following the play, as left tackle Trent Williams was seen slamming his helmet on the ground after the injury happened.

Before McKivitz was carted off the field, McCaffrey was one of the last 49ers players to talk to him, and that's when the 49ers running back was consoling the offensive lineman.

49ers' offensive lineman Colton McKivitz was carted off the field during Sunday's game against the Vikings. AP Photo/Eduardo Verdugo

When the game ended, McCaffrey told reporters "[He's one of the heart and souls of this team. He is the standard and we're gonna miss him. We all love him, and we're praying for him," McCaffrey told reporters following the game. "It's one of the worst parts of this game, is stuff like that."

McKivitz is in his seventh year in the NFL and has turned into a franchise right tackle after the 49ers selected him in the fifth round of the 2020 NFL Draft.

This season, he's played a vital part in the success of McCaffrey, and quarterback Brock Purdy has been among the fewest-sacked starting quarterbacks this year.

McCaffrey has run the ball 127 times through 10 games this season and is averaging 4.5 yards per rush with 11 touchdowns.
