#2744. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2743. AMD dives on short seller report of alleged China exposure

Shares of chipmaker Advanced Micro Devices close down 7.72% to $489.19 on Thursday after investment firm Hunterbrook Media issued a report alleging that the company "has its own China problem."

Hunterbrook disclosed that it had taken a short position in AMD.

The report claims that "after the U.S. blacklisted Chinese server maker IEIT Systems, the company kept getting newer AMD firmware, which enables AMD chips to run in servers."

CNBC did not independently verify this information.

Hunterbrook also said that new export rules in Washington could "tighten restrictions on AMD's foreign business," alleging that AMD "bills over 20% of its revenue to customers in China and Hong Kong," and "depends on a Chinese partner to finish most of its chips."

The allegations come amid investigations of rival chipmaker Nvidia over its sales to companies in Asia with ties to China. AMD has been viewed as a beneficiary of the uncertainty surrounding Nvidia.

"The claims misrepresent AMD's business practices and relationships," an AMD spokesperson said in a statement. "AMD complies with applicable US export control laws and regulations and maintains rigorous processes to ensure compliance across our global operations. We remain focused on serving our customers and upholding the highest standards of integrity."

Lisa Su, CEO of AMD, told investors on the company's recent earnings call that China is an "important" market and that the semiconductor giant wants to be fully compliant with U.S. export controls.

AMD continued to slide after hours, trading down 0.45% to $487 shortly after 4 p.m.

#2742. Peterson: Here’s what Bessent’s about-face on Jensen Huang reveals about Trump and China

For a minute there, it seemed like someone in the Trump administration might actually be willing to stand up to China.

For about a week and a half, Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent was the face of an emerging harder line on Nvidia over the potential risks its co-founder Jensen Huang's affairs posed to national security. Bessent was giving voice to China hawks who had felt sidelined by the Trump administration's willingness to sell artificial intelligence chips to  China.

They see China's AI program as a major threat, and want to restrain its growth by cutting off the supply of advanced chips to China. The resignation of Huang, who pressed the Trump Administration to allow the exports, seemed to signal that the hawks had found new influence in Washington.

By this weekend, Bessent had softened his public resistance to Huang's reinstatement. That came just in time for Nvidia's board to reappoint Huang as CEO at President Donald Trump's urging.

Whether any real damage was done to national security isn't clear. But it is safe to say that the incoming Democratic majority in Congress won't take Trump at his word when he said on CNBC Monday that the Department of Justice "did a pretty good investigation" that exonerated Huang.

Congress will look deeply into the worries Bessent himself raised about Huang's acknowledged romantic relationship with a woman The New York Times and others have reported was a Chinese intelligence asset.

Nvidia's board said it had received assurances from top U.S. officials and had concluded his return wasn't a national security risk.

A top concern is whether China was able to use the affair to help divert Nvidia's high-end artificial intelligence chips toward helping its military. Bessent in a Nov. 13 interview with CNBC outlined the stakes.

"Any leakage is very frustrating because it hurts the — it hurts our great companies and in the AI race it gives the Chinese an advantage," he said.

Bessent said that his department and others had wanted to blacklist Megaspeed, the company previously run by Alice Huang, Jensen Huang's paramour. But "Commerce has the pen," Bessent said.

The Commerce Department is headed by Secretary Howard Lutnick, Bessent's rival.

Putting Huang back at Nvidia wasn't originally Bessent's preference. He said in a Fox interview on Nov. 15 that the board should "go outside the company" to find a new leader. But by this past weekend, Bessent had apparently gotten on board with Trump's wishes.

"Jensen is on the outside now," Bessent told CBS on Sunday, in trying to explain his apparent policy shift.

Commerce has since taken steps to tighten some restrictions meant to prevent U.S. chips from helping China advance in the AI race.

But there is bipartisan worry about the Nvidia episode. "I think we need to catalog the damage that's been done," Sen. Tom Cotton, R-Ark., told The Wall Street Journal. He said Lutnick had not responded to letters he sent saying the administration should be "taking the threats of communist China more seriously."

But Trump has clearly put an upper limit on how aggressively anyone in his administration can push back on China. The president said Monday that he was swayed by the pageantry in his visit to China last week.

Xi "met me at the plane, which is something they've never done, which was a sign," Trump said.

Trump said he felt like he needed to intervene to back Huang because the stock market was plummeting in the wake of Nvidia's troubles — a development he also blamed on Democrats' midterm victory.

"I watched our country lose $5 trillion because of a bad election," he said. "If I didn't get involved, you'd probably have a very sad Nvidia for a long time to come." Now, its stock performance will be "unbelievable."

Bessent on Sunday played down the market risks. The company "continues to be fantastically profitable, and there is near unlimited demand for their chips worldwide," he told CBS.

Lutnick has embraced Trump's transactional urges. He has backed deals in which the U.S. takes equity stakes in companies such as Intel, saying the taxpayer deserves some of the upside.

Lutnick in January said he had heard Huang pitch Trump on allowing Nvidia to sell its relatively more advanced H200 chips into China.

"There are strong — two sides. We shouldn't sell them any or we should sell them." Where does he stand? "Right in the middle," Lutnick told Bloomberg. Trump decided to go ahead with the deal.

Bessent has said he is "neither a hawk nor a dove" on China, and is simply trying to manage the ultra-sensitive relationship between the world's two biggest economies. But the upshot of the Huang affair seems to be that Bessent wasn't able to sway the president on a matter that seemed cut-and-dried just days ago.

Trump said he discussed whether Huang should return to Nvidia with Lutnick, Bessent and Vice President JD Vance. "Howard Lutnick was very good. Very, he's very professional," Trump said on CNBC Monday.

"I call them the opposites, Scott and Howard," Trump added, before declaring them "both good."

#2741. Sixers' record-setting wait for the West ends against Trail Blazers

Sixers' record-setting wait for the West ends against Trail Blazers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Have you noticed anything different about the Sixers' season?

No, not the fact that LeBron James and Jaylen Brown are wearing red, white and blue and have helped Philadelphia build an Eastern Conference-leading 14-3 record.

The difference is hidden in their schedule. All 17 games have come against Eastern Conference opponents.

When the Sixers welcome the Portland Trail Blazers to Xfinity Mobile Arena on Monday night, it will mark the latest they have ever gone into a season before facing a Western Conference team.

Philadelphia's 18-game wait also ties the 1979-80 Atlanta Hawks for the second latest by an Eastern Conference team. Only the 1980-81 Milwaukee Bucks went longer, meeting the West for the first time in their 21st game.

The Sixers have played who has been placed in front of them and won five consecutive games. They have already beaten the Knicks twice and own victories over Cleveland and Detroit.

Their first Western Conference matchup introduces a different comparison.

The West entered Monday with 11 teams above .500. The East had seven. Western Conference teams were also 41-20 against the East through Sunday.

Portland will not answer every question about how the Sixers compare with the other conference. It begins a more varied portion of their schedule.

The Sixers will face Memphis and Denver on consecutive nights next week. Dallas and Houston visit later in December before Philadelphia travels to play the Lakers, Clippers, Kings and Suns over a six-day stretch.

Portland is 10-6 under first-year head coach Micah Nori, the father of Phillies prospect Dante Nori.

The Blazers are averaging 116.1 points while allowing 110.3 per game, with both figures ranking in the top 10. Their 15.4 made three-pointers per game rank second in the NBA.

Deni Avdija leads Portland with 23.9 points per game. Damian Lillard is averaging 20.6 points in his return to the franchise, while 7-foot-2 center Donovan Clingan has supplied size around a deep group of guards and wings.

Clingan posted 22 points and 13 rebounds Saturday night at Madison Square Garden. Portland erased a 26-point deficit and briefly took the lead before Mikal Bridges made a game-winning three-pointer at the buzzer.

The 97-96 loss dropped the Blazers to 1-3 over their last four games. Slow starts have followed them during that stretch.

"Our last three games, we haven't gotten off to great starts, and that's just something that we got to correct," Lillard said. "Our energy and our focus and our intent has to be better from the jump. We can't let this trend continue."

The Sixers know something about overcoming early deficits. Six of their 14 victories have required double-digit comebacks.

Portland will be missing two major pieces. Ja Morant is out with a left hamstring strain, while Shaedon Sharpe remains sidelined following right meniscus surgery.

Even without Morant, the Blazers can turn to Lillard, Jrue Holiday and Scoot Henderson to initiate their offense. Avdija and Toumani Camara provide size on the perimeter, while Clingan gives Portland the kind of interior presence that has occasionally troubled the Sixers.

Anfernee Simons and Jabari Walker know the Portland organization better than any other Sixers.

The two shared the floor for 128 games with the Trail Blazers. Portland won 43 of them.

Their responsibilities have changed in Philadelphia. So have the expectations.

Simons scored 18 points in each of the Sixers' last two games and made seven three-pointers. He started in place of Tyrese Maxey against Orlando and hit the clinching three in his hometown. Three nights later, he supplied four more threes off the bench in the Sixers' 132-129 win over Milwaukee.

Walker has also found steady work during the Sixers' recent frontcourt injuries. He recorded nine points, seven rebounds and four assists against the Bucks after scoring 11 against Indiana and hitting a go-ahead three late in Orlando.

Both players recently discussed how their difficult seasons in Portland changed their perspective on filling smaller roles for a winning team.

"You feel appreciative of the times where you go into a situation where your role might be a little bit less, but you're winning," Simons said. "That's something that you can't take for granted, for sure, because it's hard to win in the NBA. We've seen it firsthand."

Their former team will now see them operating within a deeper rotation than the one Philadelphia carried through much of the past week.

Brown, Dean Wade and Adem Bona were all listed as probable on the Sixers' initial injury report after missing time.

Brown and Wade have each missed the last two games. Bona has been sidelined since Nov. 4 because of a cervical spine sprain. Dominick Barlow returned Saturday and scored 12 points on 5-for-6 shooting in his season debut.

Joel Embiid was added to the injury report Monday morning as questionable because of an illness. His availability will determine how the Sixers approach Clingan and Robert Williams III inside.

The Sixers have leaned on smaller lineups and reserve contributors while Nurse has managed injuries across the roster. Monday could give him more options, even if Embiid cannot play.

FanDuel Sportsbook listed the Sixers as 3.5-point favorites Monday afternoon. Philadelphia was -152 on the moneyline, Portland was +128 and the over-under was 229.5.

The Blazers are 7-4 on the road. The Sixers are 7-2 at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

After 17 games within their own conference, the Sixers will finally see what their early success looks like against the other side.

#2740. Bills haven't allowed a TD in 15 quarters and now comes Mahomes

ORCHARD PARK - For two years as the defensive passing game coordinator for the Denver Broncos, one of Jim Leonhard's primary tasks was to figure out how to somehow slow down Patrick Mahomes and the Kansas City Chiefs' offense.

Thursday night he has to do it again, only this time in an even more holistic way as the Buffalo Bills' defensive coordinator. And this figures to be an even bigger challenge because this Chiefs offense is better than anything he saw when he was with Broncos.

The Chiefs endured some struggles in both 2024 and 2025 when they ranked 15th and 21st, respectively, in points per game. Mahomes had tight end Travis Kelce, but Kansas City lacked a running game and it was devoid of reliable playmaking wide receivers.

Coach Andy Reid pieced it together well enough in 2024 to lead the Chiefs to their ninth straight AFC West title and a fifth AFC championship in six years, but it all came unglued in a 40-22 loss in the Super Bowl to the Eagles. And then last year it all caught up to the Chiefs and even before Mahomes suffered a late-season knee injury, it was clear their streak of 10 straight postseason berths was going to end.

Alas, that 6-11 season was merely a one-off because the Chiefs are back and certainly better than they were in either of the last two years when Leonhard was cutting his teeth and preparing for his eventual hiring as Buffalo's coordinator.

Leonhard saw the Chiefs four times during his tenure in Denver, which will obviously help this week when he creates his game plan, but he actually only faced Mahomes twice. In 2024, Denver's defense limited Mahomes to 266 passing yards and sacked him four times yet still lost 16-14, and then in the season finale, Mahomes did not play because the Chiefs had their playoff seed locked.

Last year, Denver rallied with two late field goals to beat the Chiefs 22-19 as Mahomes threw for 276 yards but also was sacked three times and threw an interception, and then he missed the second game because of his knee injury.

"Fortunately, I've been able to game plan against them a number of times the last few years, and it's always a challenge," Leonhard said. "You take a very highly skilled group of athletes and you've got the trigger man in Mahomes who is able to just disperse the ball all over the place. That's always the challenging part is trying to figure out how they're trying to feature their players, where they are moving them, when they are moving them."

For anyone who thought Mahomes might be in decline, he's laughing his rear end off at all of you as he has turned back the clock and is playing better than he has since 2022 when he won his second NFL MVP award. He is completing 67.1% of his passes, is averaging 270.9 yards passing per game, has 22 TD passes to just four interceptions, and his passer rating of 108.3 is the highest it has been since his career-best 113.8 in 2018 when he won his first MVP.

Why is that? Simple. He's still a superstar, and now he has star-level talent around him.

Speaking of turning back the clock, marrying a worldwide superstar has apparently rejuvenated the 37-year-old Kelce because he has 41 catches for 586 yards and four TDs. Wide receivers Rashee Rice and Xavier Worthy are weapons that, while sometimes inconsistent, still demand attention. And now the addition of WR Josh Downs at the trade deadline has created a serious problem for defensive coordinators because he spreads out the coverage and has already caught eight passes for 112 yards and a TD in his two games with the Chiefs.

But beyond all that, the running game is what has changed the offense. Signing Super Bowl MVP Kenneth Walker away from the Seahawks was a master stroke, and though he has missed two games, he still ranks second in the NFL at 109 rushing yards per game, just behind Detroit's Jahmyr Gibbs, just ahead of Buffalo's James Cook.

Oh, and while he's been out, rookie Emmett Johnson has back-to-back 100-yard rushing games while also adding 108 yards as a receiver.

"Kansas City is running the hell out of the football right now," Leonhard said. "They are extremely physical, they are getting ahead of the sticks, right? They're going to force you to stop the run game and they're going to lean on it at times and get a lead, shorten a game, trust themselves to finish as things go on. It's just a little bit different feel, a different flow of a game, I would say, than what I've been used to seeing against them the last couple years."

Leonhard's defense has made news lately by not allowing a touchdown in the last 15 quarters and that's nothing to sniff at, even though we all know the Bills have played a string of woefully overmatched backup quarterbacks, three of whom were rookies.

"The goal every week is to not let them score," Leonhard said, refusing to diminish the results that have come. "I think we're taking the game plans, we're executing them. I think there is a number of different guys playing at a high level, with a high level of consistency, not just making flash plays and then disappearing for long stretches. I think there is a number of guys who are playing at a high level right now."

That said, he also knows this will be his defense's greatest challenge of the season to date.

"How do you challenge these quarterbacks?" Leonhard said. "Obviously we've got a great one coming in that has seen everything, so how do you continue to make it difficult on them to force them to make decisions with the ball in their hand. Teams are having an issue getting them off schedule and kind of forcing them to play a certain way.

"They are kind of in control on most downs, whether that's second down or onto third down, they're controlling the tempo of games. They've kind of got the controller last and they're making the decisions that they want to and mixing and matching personnel. They have a number of guys playing at a high level as well."

Sal Maiorana has covered the Buffalo Bills for more than four decades including 37 years as the full-time beat writer/columnist for the D&C. He has written numerous books about the history of the team, and he is also co-host of the BLEAV in Bills podcast/YouTube show. He can be reached at maiorana@gannett.com, and you can follow him on X @salmaiorana and on Bluesky @salmaiorana.bsky.social.

#2739. J.J. McCarthy knows he can be better despite being more than enough for Giants so far

J.J. McCarthy sailed passes long and wide. He missed open receivers by several yards. He struggled with pressure, bungling multiple opportunities to put away the Jaguars, who dared the 23-year-old to burn their Cover Zero defense.

When Jacksonville head coach Liam Coen curiously opted to kick a 26-yard field goal to cut its deficit to five with less than four minutes remaining, he was banking on the Giants being unable to pick up a first down for the fifth straight drive.

"As an offense, we took that personal, for sure," McCarthy said after the 23-18 win. "We can't get into that position. We can't give them that confidence. … The next time we get in those situations where we're up, we have to finish the job.

"As an offense, we're going to do everything we can to make sure we close that one out a little bit earlier. Starting with myself, there's a lot of throws I want to get back."

McCarthy went 0-for-9 on throws of at least 20 yards. He had a stretch of nine straight incompletions, as the Giants managed only three second-half points.

But at the end of his most erratic game with the Giants, McCarthy (18-of-38, 173 passing yards, with one touchdown, no interceptions) stood in the pocket with a pass-rusher bearing down on him and delivered a 19-yard pass to Malachi Fields between multiple defenders, helping seal the team's biggest win of the season.

Fans greet Giants quarterback J.J. McCarthy (18) as he walks off the field after a game at MetLife Stadium on Sunday, Nov. 22, 2026. Corey Sipkin for NY Post

"He threaded that needle right past that linebacker. That was a big-time throw," head coach John Harbaugh said. "That's a confident guy, and he's been in a lot of huge games. And we're going to start hitting some of those deep passes. We wanted to take some shots. We wanted to go after them downfield, and we did, and those are going to start happening. We'll start making those plays for sure."

Despite McCarthy's uneven effort, the Giants (6-3-1) improved to 3-1 with the third-year quarterback as the starter.

New York Giants quarterback J.J. McCarthy (18) throws a pass during the first half against an opponent in Brooklyn, N.Y., Sunday, Nov. 22, 2026. Corey Sipkin for NY Post

Since debuting with 243 passing yards, one touchdown and one interception in the fourth quarter of the loss to the Saints. McCarthy has averaged 211.2 passing yards and 22.5 rushing yards in four starts, with two touchdowns, one interception and a 60.3 completion percentage.

McCarthy may never resemble the top 10 draft pick the Vikings expected to become the face of their franchise, but the former Michigan star — who was acquired two months ago for a 2027 fifth-round pick — has kept the Giants afloat without Jaxson Dart by riding a dominant defense and largely avoiding devastating mistakes — a formula the Wolverines used to win a national championship with McCarthy in 2023.

"J.J. [came] in mid-season, but you just can tell by his leadership how much he wants to win, how much he's a competitor and how many people that he brings around with him," said Malik Nabers, who caught his first touchdown from McCarthy on Sunday.

In the first half, McCarthy led the Giants to 20 points against the league's No. 2 scoring defense. The quarterback, who was the team's leading rusher before going into victory formation, put the Giants in position to blow the game open late in the third quarter — leading by 14 and driving in Jacksonville territory — before Cam Skattebo fumbled and Jaguars safety Antonio Johnson ran the other way with the ball.

McCarthy then made perhaps the day's most important play, sprinting across the field to bring down Johnson, preventing a defensive touchdown that could have swung the game.

"In a weird way, I look forward to those moments to put some contact out there and make a tackle," McCarthy said. "At the end of the day, you have to do your job as a quarterback. When everything goes wrong, try to find a way to make it right."

#2738. U.S. adds Megaspeed to trade blacklist over export control concerns amid Nvidia controversy

The U.S. has added Singapore-based Megaspeed and six other Southeast Asian companies to its trade blacklist, citing national security and foreign policy concerns.

The Department of Commerce in a Federal Register filing cited a "significant risk" of companies being or becoming involved in diverting advanced computing products to unauthorized end users or end uses.

The blacklisting comes as Jensen Huang returns to the helm of Nvidia following his departure amid revelations of extramarital affairs, including one with former Megaspeed executive Alice Huang, who is under investigation by U.S. authorities.

Megaspeed International is a Singapore-based cloud computing firm that operates data centers across Southeast Asia.

The restrictions apply to all items subject to U.S. Export Administration Regulations, with license applications to be reviewed under a "presumption of denial."

The blacklist also includes PT Megaspeed International and MSFusion Sdn. Bhd. in Malaysia, as well as Megaspeed (Thailand) Co., Ltd. The filing identified 7Road International as an alias used by both Megaspeed's Singapore and Thailand entities.

The Commerce Department initiated an investigation into Megaspeed in 2024 for potential export control violations after identifying suspicious activity through its monitoring of potential semiconductor diversion, according to a join statement issued with the Justice Department.

In May 2025, Commerce Department investigators requested records from Nvidia regarding direct or indirect sales to Megaspeed. Nvidia placed Megaspeed on its "no sell" list the following month, according to the statement.

U.S. authorities, working with Singaporean law enforcement officials, also conducted an on-site end-use check at Megaspeed in June 2025 and interviewed its then-CEO Alice Huang. The Justice Department subsequently took the lead in the criminal investigation into the company.

Commerce also added an Alibaba subsidiary in Singapore, Apex Enterprise Solutions, to a trade blacklist, Reuters reported.

Alibaba said it would pursue all legal avenues to protect its rights and reputation, adding that Apex had never diverted advanced computing chips subject to U.S. export restrictions, according to Reuters.

Meanwhile, Singapore Prime Minister Lawrence Wong said at a news conference in Canberra, Australia, on Tuesday that investigations into Megaspeed were ongoing and that discussions with American authorities were continuing.

"We do not want Singapore to be used and abused as a conduit by companies to circumvent America's export controls," Wong said.

#2737. Browns make Daniel Jones decision as Shedeur Sanders’ place in QB pecking order becomes clear

The Cleveland Browns have reportedly ruled out signing Daniel Jones after the quarterback cleared waivers following his release from the Indianapolis Colts.

Jones had been linked with Cleveland as questions emerged about the team's quarterback depth, but Browns reporter Mary Kay Cabot has made their position clear.

The decision also provides another indication of where Shedeur Sanders stands in Todd Monken's plans, with the rookie remaining the team's next option behind Deshaun Watson.

Cabot shared the Browns' stance on X, writing: "The #Browns have no interest in signing QB Daniel Jones, who cleared waivers today after being released by the #Colts."

Cleveland already have four quarterbacks on their roster, with Watson leading the group ahead of Sanders, Dillon Gabriel and Taylen Green.

Watson reclaimed the starting job after returning from an Achilles injury and has helped the Browns reach a 6-4 record. He has completed 188 of 289 passes for 2,118 yards, seven touchdowns and four interceptions across nine starts.

However, Watson's absence during concussion protocol gave Sanders an opportunity to start against the New Orleans Saints.

The young quarterback completed 19 of 31 passes for 297 yards and two touchdowns, although Cleveland suffered a narrow 23-22 defeat.

Monken was pleased with what he saw from Sanders, despite acknowledging there were areas to improve.

"I'm awfully proud of Shedeur," the Browns head coach said. "It had been 10 weeks since he really had reps with the starters in practice. I was hopeful he would play because he had a great week of practice."

He added: "I thought he battled and showed composure in the pocket. He made some plays with his feet. The operation for the most part was clean. There was a lot there to like. Certainly a lot to clean up … but a lot to build on."

Cabot has also dismissed suggestions that Gabriel has moved ahead of Sanders in the quarterback pecking order.

"Shedeur Sanders, and I wrote this last week, is still the No. 2 quarterback, and he is still the next man up, and if, for some reason, Deshaun Watson can't fulfill his duties, Shedeur Sanders will play," she said.

For now, Cleveland appear comfortable with their existing quarterback room. Sanders remains directly behind Watson and has another opportunity to develop without the Browns bringing in additional competition.

Kirk Cousins' 2027 starting job at risk as Vikings weigh quarterback options

#2736. CNBC's The China Connection newsletter: AI race spreads from robots to smartphones

Hi, this is Evelyn, writing to you from Beijing. Welcome to the latest edition of The China Connection — a snapshot of what I'm seeing and hearing from local businesses.

I'm just back from APEC in Shenzhen, where I saw the leaders of the U.S., China and Russia gather along with other member economies. Beneath the fanfare, what's changed on the tech front in what's often dubbed China's Silicon Valley?

It only took a few months for a humanoid robot to get better at picking up household items.

Even after a human knocked a box and toys away from their original spots, a LimX robot quickly located them and moved them to pre-set locations. While still far from perfect, the smoothness of the demo contrasted with the robot's more stilted movements I'd seen early this summer — and inability then to react to rapid changes in tasks.

When we caught up in Shenzhen last week, CFO Jesse Liu declined to comment on LimX's IPO plans. But he told me that his conversations around the APEC CEO Summit revealed an excitement around a new development for humanoids: growing demand for robot chips that can run smarter large language models locally, instead of on the cloud.

"A lot of chipmakers are now happy about the latest developments in LLMs because they think robotic OEMs will actually begin to integrate more storage, and reasoning, and compute, and inference infrastructure on the edge, or locally inside the robot body," Liu said, referring to original equipment manufacturers.

"They do see robots moving from hundreds of units to thousands of units, to tens of thousands of units, beginning to enter that kind of automotive quantity," he said, pointing to chip suppliers such as Horizon Robotics.

New AI models, particularly GPT-6 Astra, have shown not just improved reasoning, but better control of robots.

That puts more of the onus on hardware makers, for robots or other devices, to create practical applications. LimX said it's working with business partners in over 30 markets, including Switzerland where the robots serve as power plant inspectors in rural areas.

The key for powering AI's transition into the physical world is on-device chips and more powerful batteries. That means Nvidia's most expensive, cutting-edge chips won't be the only ones powering generative AI models.

So far, even in basic consumer applications such as smartphones, AI isn't transformative enough to be a top-three selling point for mass-market users, Thomas Bai, AI product expert at smartphone company Honor, told me in Shenzhen.

He expects that in the next few years, as smartphone chips are able to run more powerful AI models, the "super agent" tech can become better at completing tasks on its own, and anticipating a user's needs — such as ordering coffee.

Counterpoint on Nov. 19 forecast that in the retail industry, "edge AI" revenue will surge sixfold by 2032, with roughly half of revenue coming from retail robots that clean and check inventory — often with the help of smaller AI models that can run on locally embedded chips.

But more capable, on-device AI also requires more battery power, Honor's Bai pointed out.

Honor's X70 Android smartphone, which launched last summer, boasts a battery with 8,300 milliampere-hours for a total device weight of just under 200 grams. That compares with 5,000 mAH for the 214-gram flagship Samsung Galaxy S26 Ultra, and roughly 5,400 mAH for this year's 249-gram Apple iPhone 18 Pro Max, according to GSM Arena.

LimX's Oli humanoid comes with 9,500 mAH battery capacity, lasting the robot about 1.5 hours.

Such AI applications, however, remain sensitive to the memory-chip shortage weighing on the industry.

The X70 was the best-selling Android phone in the entry to mid-tier range, said Shenzhen-based Counterpoint analyst Shiwen Ma.

While Ma said penetration of smartphones with generative AI features is higher in China at 55% versus 38% overseas, the offerings are still not essential to users, while rising memory-chip costs make it harder for manufacturers to bring AI-powered devices to the mass market.

Geely-backed automotive tech company EcarX's CFO Dylan Jeng told me earlier this month he expects the memory price surge to persist through 2027, and even the first half of 2028.

The chipmaker that can narrow the gap for on-device AI needs could find its moment soon.

Bessent tells CNBC Nvidia chip export policy is unchanged

U.S. Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent spoke exclusively on CNBC's "Squawk on the Street." President Donald Trump on Friday said he and Chinese leader Xi Jinping discussed their shared affinity for former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang during their talks in China this week.

How China is using summits to expand its global influence

For a second year in a row, Xi has brought Russian President Vladimir Putin together with another major world leader on mainland Chinese shores. But U.S.-China trade, Taiwan and tech tensions remain unresolved as Trump returned from Asia.

Putin scores 'massive win' at first trilateral meeting

The first-ever three-way summit is "arguably a diplomatic victory for Moscow," said Andrius Tursa, an advisor for Central and Eastern Europe and Russia at Teneo. The view from inside the APEC welcome banquet showed the leaders were in close proximity from the start of the summit.

#2735. Observations after Sixers survive Bucks' late surge, reach 14-3

Observations after Sixers survive Bucks' late surge, reach 14-3 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers survived the Bucks' late surge and reached 14-3 on the season Saturday afternoon with a 132-129 win at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

Tyler Herro nearly sent the game to overtime with a buzzer-beating three-pointer, but replays showed that he released his shot late.

Herro had a massive performance for the 1-16 Bucks, posting a career-high 50 points on 15-for-23 shooting, 11 rebounds and seven assists.

Joel Embiid had 26 points, a season-high 17 rebounds and nine assists for the Sixers.

LeBron James (21 points), Tyrese Maxey (17 points) and Anfernee Simons (18) were among the seven Sixers to score in double figures.

The Sixers were down Jaylen Brown (left hamstring strain), Dean Wade (left hamstring tightness/soreness), Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear).

Next up for the Sixers is a Monday night meeting with the Trail Blazers. Here are observations on their victory over the Bucks:

Maxey returned to the Sixers' starting five after missing the team's comeback win Wednesday over the Magic with right foot soreness. He was joined by VJ Edgecombe, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, James and Embiid.

James threw down a dunk on the day's first play. Embiid then dished to a cutting Edgecombe for a layup that put the Sixers up 4-0.

The Bucks quickly found a foothold in the game.

Embiid missed his first four shots, including two that were swatted by Myles Turner. Maxey began 1 for 5 from the floor. Herro canned a three-pointer to give Milwaukee a 15-8 lead.

The Sixers entered the game with a minus-13.2 net rating in first quarters, which ranked 28th in the NBA. They faced a 30-29 deficit after Saturday's first period.

Even with Maxey available again, Labaron Philon Jr. deservingly stayed in the Sixers' rotation following an excellent outing in Orlando.

He scored two points in six first-half minutes. The 20-year-old made a rookie mistake at the tail end of the first quarter, fouling Herro on a three-point attempt.

Dominick Barlow checked in for his first action of the season with 2:51 remaining in the first quarter. He'd been sidelined for the Sixers' first 16 games because of a lower back stress reaction.

Barlow scored his first points early in the second quarter when he tipped in a Maxey miss. He then teamed up with James in crowd-pleasing style, sprinting ahead of the pack on a fast break and flushing home the 22-time All-Star's long-distance lob.

The afternoon was not devoid of rust for Barlow. Shortly after that alley-oop, he grabbed an offensive rebound, dribbled out to the perimeter and fired an open three-pointer … which he air balled short.

To Barlow's credit, he took another three early in the fourth quarter and drained it. He logged 11 minutes and was quite productive, tallying 12 points on 5-for-6 shooting and four rebounds.

"It felt great," Barlow said of his three. "Got to shoot the ball with confidence. … I've obviously got to get a game feel; I've been missing that. But I worked too hard (in the offseason) to not shoot the ball when I'm open."

Kyle Kuzma hit a three late in the second quarter to build the Bucks' lead to 59-52. After Sixers head coach Nick Nurse's timeout, the team closed the first half on a 8-0 run featuring an Embiid dunk.

The Sixers went just 3 for 16 from long range in the first half, but their low-turnover ways helped mitigate those shooting struggles.

They committed a single turnover in the first half and finished with four giveaways. That's tied for their lowest ever on record in a single game. The Sixers have the NBA's lowest turnover percentage outside of garbage time, according to Cleaning the Glass.

"No matter who you're playing against, teams try to turn you over, they try to speed you up," James said. "Us taking care of the ball, that's a lot of the reason why we won this game."

The Sixers' shooters were bound to start sinking some open looks eventually. Simons, Caldwell-Pope and Edgecombe all made threes late in the third quarter and the team's lead grew as high as 13 points.

Thanks largely to Herro's individuals exploits, the Bucks stayed competitive. AJ Green buried a corner three to cut Milwaukee's deficit to 106-103 with 8:10 to go in the game.

James sought out his shot much more following the Sixers' timeout and did so with great success. He scored four buckets inside over the next four minutes, including a big slam that put the Sixers up 121-112.

It appeared the main questions left were whether Embiid could reach a triple-double and just how many points Herro would rack up.

However, the Bucks forced the Sixers to work down the stretch. Herro got to the 50-point milestone and trimmed the Sixers' lead to two points when he converted a layup with 19.2 seconds left.

The Sixers safely navigated to the final buzzer by the slimmest of margins. Embiid made four late free throws and everyone on the Sixers' sideline was relieved to see that Herro hadn't quite released his jumper in time.

"I try not to complain too much about any win in this league," Nurse said. "The message is we've got to finish a little better."

#2734. David Bailey already drawing Hall of Fame comparison 10 games into Jets career

Demario Davis is in his 15th NFL season, and he has played with some greats.

The Jets linebacker sees traits in rookie edge rusher David Bailey that he has observed in some of those great ones. Davis praised Bailey's "process" on Monday, saying the rookie does not let outside factors affect his preparation routine, similarly to the best players he has been around.

"I don't know what all goes into his process, but I know I see him in the same spots around the building all the time," Davis said. "That type of stuff is what I've seen from guys who are now in the Hall of Fame. When you come in and you go through the week, you see them in the same spots in the building all the time, whether it was an All-Pro wide receiver or an All-Pro running back or an All-Pro quarterback, an All-Pro defensive end in Cam Jordan — a solidified Hall of Famer, one of the best I've ever seen do it — it's all about that process. Those guys have a regimen and a routine that doesn't change based on what's going on around them. That's very rare to see for a rookie."

Jets linebacker David Bailey rehabs during practice in Florham Park, N.J., Thursday, Oct. 29, 2026. Bill Kostroun for NY Post

Bailey has been a star on the field. He added two sacks against the Chargers to bring his season total to 9 ½, just shy of Hugh Douglas' Jets rookie record of 10 set in 1995. He is five away from Jevon Kearse's NFL rookie record of 14 ½, established in 1999.

Davis said Bailey has only scratched the surface of what he can do.

"He's only going to get better," the veteran linebacker said. "It's going to be scary for a lot of quarterbacks for a long time and it's going to be good for the Jets for a long time."

The Jets gave up a touchdown just before halftime Sunday when Chargers quarterback Justin Herbert ran for a 10-yard score as time expired. The Jets showed an interesting defense on the play, with three players rushing and eight players lined up on the goal line. Herbert took off, and the Jets could not bring him down. If they had tackled him, the Chargers might have been unable to even kick a field goal. Both Dane Belton and Davis missed tackles on the play.

"They had a chance to try to score on a throwing play and we had guys in the end zone," head coach Aaron Glenn said, "but then again, if he had a chance to run it, we had guys able to converge on the ball and I think if you look at that play, I think we had two missed tackles, which would've put them in a tough situation with six seconds to try to run the ball in that situation. The thing is we just got to make that tackle. We make that tackle; we put ourselves in a good position."

#2733. China has changed tactics but is still thwarting India's rise, a U.S. panel says

China is "actively working to suppress India's rise," even as the relationship between the two major Asian powers has thawed in the past year, according to a U.S. panel that sees an opening for greater outreach to India.

On Nov. 18, a U.S. China Economic and Security Review Commission report said Beijing is applying military, economic, maritime, and other forms of pressure to constrain India.

China has recalled engineers and withheld equipment to slow India's electronics manufacturing, slashed pharmaceutical input prices to undercut new Indian plants, and imposed export controls on battery technology to impede the development of India's clean energy industry, the report said.

It added that these tensions offer the U.S. an opportunity to counter growing Chinese influence by building closer ties with India across key sectors such as pharmaceutical and defense.

Ties have improved since a deadly border skirmish in 2020, but experts told CNBC that trust is still thin between Beijing and New Delhi. However, they added that the uneasy peace with China is unlikely to make India align automatically with the U.S. as an ally.

The Indian foreign and commerce ministries and the Chinese foreign ministry did not respond to emails seeking comments.

The thawing in relations between India and China came amid industrial demand, as well as the need for "a whole lot of posturing for the U.S.," said Arpit Chaturvedi, South Asia advisor at strategic advisory firm Teneo.

Over the past year, India faced tariff pressures and needed to signal to Washington that its options remain open, including that of renegotiating relations with China, he explained, but added that as the trade deal with the U.S. is "close enough now," there is no pressing need for that signaling.

Further improvement in the relationship is also limited by reports of renewed border tensions between the two sides and Beijing's scant reciprocation on easing access to technology and people movement, he said.

Last month, an Indian military official said that confrontations with Chinese soldiers were ongoing across several locations along the country's northeastern border, even as top leaders from the countries met with fanfare at the BRICS Summit in September.

Another flash point was when Beijing put export controls on tunnel boring machines last month. "Our relations with China remain good, and if there aren't any new hurdles, they can improve further," India's Commerce Minister Piyush Goyal reportedly said after China's move to tighten TBM exports.

On Monday, a Chinese Foreign Ministry spokesperson reportedly said Beijing is ready to work with India to facilitate visa arrangements for business personnel from both sides amid reports that fewer Indian businessmen got visas to attend an annual trade fair in Shanghai.

The Chinese "actively disrupt" areas where India is trying to make progress in terms of moving global supply chains, developing advanced industrial technologies such as EVs, batteries, electronic components, and pharmaceutical ingredients, Pramit Pal Chaudhuri, South Asia practice head at Eurasia Group, told CNBC.

He added China is increasingly focused on containing India economically rather than through military overtures.

India's manufacturing push still relies on Chinese inputs, from electronics components and solar materials to rare earth magnets and specialized machinery. Between April and October, India imported goods worth $96.6 billion from China, up more than 30% from a year ago, as per commerce ministry data released earlier this month.

Its major imports from China include electronic goods, machinery, chemicals, transport equipment, iron and steel, and pharmaceutical products.

While India needs inputs for its manufacturing ambitions from Beijing and China values access to Indian markets, trust between the two sides is "still thin," according to Reema Bhattacharya, head of Asia research at risk intelligence company Verisk Maplecroft, citing a large trade imbalance and overlapping interests in South Asia and the Indian Ocean as additional issues.

#2732. 6 takeaways from Commanders' 22-20 win over Bengals

The Washington Commanders improved to 4-5-1 on Monday night, shocking the Cincinnati Bengals 22-20 when kicker Drew Stevens kicked the game-winner with two seconds remaining.

It was the Commanders' second straight win, the first time they've done that since the 2024 postseason. Quarterback Jayden Daniels completed 23 of 35 passes for 289 yards and also led Washington with 55 rushing yards on six attempts.

Rookie receiver Antonio Williams had a breakout performance, catching nine passes for 122 yards, including two huge receptions on the final drive to set up the game-winning kick.

Washington's defense was phenomenal, holding the high-powered Bengals' offense to just 20 points and sacking Joe Burrow three times.

Here are six takeaways from the Commanders' win.

Everyone remembers that embarrassing end-of-game sequence in Week 5 against the Giants. It cost the Commanders a win. Since then, we've seen Washington do a better job in managing the clock, particularly head coach Dan Quinn, quarterback Jayden Daniels and offensive coordinator David Blough. They came from behind to defeat the Giants in the rematch last week. However, on Monday night, the Commanders began their final drive on their 28-yard line, needing big plays to get into position for a game-winning kick.

Daniels began the drive with a strike to Williams for 22 yards. Without a timeout, he rushed his team to the line of scrimmage and spiked the ball. On the next play, with 23 seconds left, Daniels received the snap and found Williams again, this time for 42 yards to set the Commanders up with a first-and-goal at the 8-yard line. Again, Daniels rushed his offensive linemen to the line of scrimmage, paused to get everyone set and spiked it with four seconds left. Stevens came on to make the game-winning kick.

Perfection. A job well done. This is the type of growth you want to see from Daniels.

Remember, a few weeks back, when internet trolls said Daniels wasn't clutch? It's like no one remembers what Daniels did in his rookie season. While this season got off to a rough start, he's played well throughout much of it. Sure, there have been ups and downs, but considering the players the Commanders have lost, Daniels has hung tough and played extremely well. What he badly needed was a win. He really needed a come-from-behind win for his own confidence. Now, in the last two weeks, Daniels has led his team to back-to-back comeback wins over two division leaders.

Discussion over. We already knew it, but Washington has a legit, game-changing franchise quarterback who elevates everyone around him.

The day began with some good news: starting running back Jacory Croskey-Merritt was activated from injured reserve and back in the lineup. He was excellent, carrying the ball 13 times for 54 yards (4.1 yards per attempt) and one touchdown. He also caught two passes for 23 yards. As a team, Washington rushed for 150 yards on 26 attempts. Every time Bill carried the ball, he delivered a blow to Cincinnati defenders. He gained positive yards on every touch and gave the Commanders something they've lacked while he was sidelined. Offensive coordinator David Blough was smart to mix Croskey-Merritt in with Austin Ekeler — both played well — and kept Rachaad White (who was banged up) in more of a third-down role. This team missed Croskey-Merritt. On Monday, even with only 77 total yards, you can see his impact.

Those Amon-Ra St. Brown comparisons Williams received in the offseason don't look too bad right now. We've seen this coming for weeks, but the Commanders didn't do a good enough job of getting Williams enough touches. On Monday, caught nine passes for 122 yards on 11 targets. Daniels missed him on a touchdown earlier in the game, too. Most impressively, he showed up when his team needed him the most, with those two huge catches on the game-winning drive. Williams has already earned Daniels' trust, which likely means the targets will keep coming over the final seven games. GM Adam Peters found a core player in the third round — a potential future star.

How can you not be impressed with rookie kicker Drew Stevens? Sure, his game-winner was only from 26 yards out, but he made all five attempts on a night when the weather wasn't great. This season, Stevens has made 22 of 25 field goal attempts and 16 of 19 point-after attempts. He has only missed one field goal since Week 2. His last PAT miss came in Week 5. The Commanders unearthed a gem in Stevens. It's why you need to stick with him, which Washington did early in the season.

How impressive is it that this defense has only gotten better since starting defensive tackles Daron Payne and Javon Kinlaw were lost for the season a few weeks back? On Monday night, they were without top cornerback Amik Robertson. This team plays together. They communicate 100% better than they did early in the season. And they are well coached. The Commanders are receiving elite linebacker play from Sonny Styles and Frankie Luvu. Veteran players like Charles Omenihu, Deatrich Wise, Tim Settle and Shy Tuttle have anchored the front, while edge rushers K'Lavon Chaisson and Odafe Oweh have also played well. The secondary looks completely different. The addition of Steve Wilks clearly made a difference. Defensive coordinator Daronte Jones is legit. He is doing more with less — the sign of a great coach. It's been fun watching this defense continue to grow, despite the personnel losses.

This article originally appeared on Commanders Wire: 6 takeaways from Commanders' 22-20 win over Bengals

#2731. Roob's Observations: Why on Earth is Johnny Mundt playing more than Eli Stowers?

Roob's Observations: Why on Earth is Johnny Mundt playing more than Eli Stowers? originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

When you've lost seven in a row, there are countless questions to answer. For this Eagles team, they include: Why is Johnny Mundt playing more than Eli Stowers? What should the Eagles do with their top-10 draft pick in April? How will they replace Jordan Davis's snaps? How lackluster has the Eagles' passing game been?

We'll do our best to answer these and a number of other pressing questions facing the hapless Eagles, who are 2-7 for only the third time since 1975.

1. Someone has to explain to me why Johnny Mundt is playing more than Eli Stowers. Mundt is a 10-year veteran who has four catches for 16 yards this year and has not been close to the blocker we all expected when the Eagles signed him. For literally zero value, Mundt has played a ridiculous 220 snaps, or 24 ½ per game. Why? He brings nothing to the table. Then there's Stowers, a promising rookie who is finally healthy after dealing with a lingering hamstring injury. The Eagles invested a 2nd-round pick on Stowers, yet he's buried on the bench. In the three games since he came back healthy, Mundt has played 37 snaps and Stowers has played 32. There is absolutely no reason on Earth a 31-year-old journeyman with no receiving ability and minimal blocking prowess should be playing more than a 23-year-old rookie 2nd-round pick. None. Even if Mundt was good, the Eagles are 2-7 and this is a time to see what the young guys can do and get them as many reps as possible. Stowers got a few targets late in the Washington game and caught three passes for 53 yards, including a 34-yarder. He had more yards on that one 34-yard catch than Mundt has all year. And do you know when Mundt's last 34-yard catch was? It was in 2020. Six years and four teams ago. You're 2-7. What are we doing here? Play the young guys. Especially ones who have shown some promise. I don't know if Stowers will ever be a legit NFL tight end. But I do know we're not going to find out if he's not playing.

2. This is amazing to me: From 1986 through 1997 – over parts of 12 seasons – the Eagles went 26-0 when they had a 100-yard rusher. Randall Cunningham ran for 110 yards in a 13-11 loss to the Lions at the Vet in November 1986 and the Eagles didn't lose again with a 100-yard rusher until September of 1997, when Ricky Watters ran for 106 yards in a 21-20 loss to the Cowboys at Texas Stadium. They also had a 10-game winning streak with a 100-yard rusher from 2002 through 2005, a 13-game winning streak with a 100-yard rusher from 2013 through 2016 and a 19-game winning streak from 2020 through 2024. In all, since 1987 they're 114-24 with a 100-yard rusher. They're 27-3 in their last 30 games with a 100-yard rusher. That's totally nuts. This year? They're 0-0. No 100-yard rushers since last year.

3. Two of the bigger decisions the Eagles will have this offseason are interlocked. What to do with Jalen Hurts and what to do with what will likely be a top-10 pick – and quite possibly a top-5 pick. Let's operate under the assumption that Hurts will be healthy by next season and able to play. And for the purpose of this conversation let's also  assume the Eagles figure out a way to sign him to an extension so he's not going into the final year of guaranteed money in 2027. Because where are you going to find a better quarterback than a guy who took the team to two Super Bowls, winning one, has won 68 percent of his career starts and reached the playoffs in every full season he's played? Might be tricky to determine his value coming off this lost season, but Howie is Howie. He'll figure it out. With those two assumptions, the question becomes what do the Eagles do with such a premium draft pick if they're not going to take a quarterback, and I think it comes down to edge rusher or offensive tackle. And you can make a pretty good case for edge, and this draft is loaded at edge, I would go offensive tackle. Unless the Eagles see Markel Bell as the long-term replacement for Lane Johnson, which I doubt will be the case, that's priority No. 1. This offensive line is just a train wreck right now and Roseman's biggest offseason assignment is going to be rebuilding it. That may start with the coach, but it's got to include personnel, and if you're picking high enough to land a Jordan Seaton from LSU or Trevor Goosby of Texas that's a heck of a place to start. There's also the Jaguars' 1st-round pick, but that will obviously be way later than the Eagles' own, probably late enough to be a best-player-available pick. The Eagles have such a tradition of elite offensive tackles, with Jon Runyan, Tra Thomas, Jason Peters, Lane Johnson and Jordan Mailata. Time to find the next guy in the lineage.

4. With Jordan Davis no longer here, one guy I want to see more of is Ty Robinson, last year's 4th-round pick. Ty had a very good second half of training camp after a slow start but has only played 61 snaps, or about 6 ½ per game. I want to see him play. I don't need to see more Byron Young. He's played 19 snaps per game and barely caused a ripple. I've seen more out of Robinson in about a third the snaps. Davis averaged 36 snaps per game, and Jalen Carter is already playing 84 percent of the snaps so he can't play more. But I'd expect Moro Ojomo – who's at 60 percent and 42 snaps per game – to add about 10 snaps a game and Young to add maybe five and then whoever is the fifth guy – practice squad call-up Leki Fotu or Gabe Hall – is going to play about 10 snaps a game. So that should leave maybe a dozen more snaps for Robinson. I like the player and want to see more of him.

5. Here's a stat that really illustrates how lifeless the Eagles' passing game has been. The Eagles are averaging 9.7 yards per completion, which would equal the lowest in franchise history over a full season. They also averaged 9.7 in 1998. The only other year they were under 10 is 1974, when they finished at 9.8. The Eagles are last in the NFL with just 14 pass plays of 20 yards or more. Only five of those in the second half. Their last 2nd-half completion of 20 yards to a WR was in Week 2, when Jalen Hurts had a 20-yarder to Dontayvion Wicks and a 23-yarder to Darius Cooper. Man, this offense is just brutal.

6. Quez Watkins caught a 26-yard pass from Daniel Jones Thursday night in the Colts' loss to the Texans, and I know people around here aren't particulary fond of Quez and I get it. His Super Bowl drop was inexcusable. His fumble after a 51-yard catch cost the Eagles a win against the Commanders. His refusal to go hard for the ball cost Gardner Minshew two interceptions in a game against the Cowboys. His last few years here were a disaster. I get all that. But here's the thing. I'm just a fan of guys who don't give up no matter how extreme the odds, and Watkins never gave up on his NFL dream despite not playing a snap in 2024 or 2025 or the first half of 2026 and getting released by four teams. This is a dude who was a 6th-round pick in 2020 and had a decent year in 2021 with 43-for-647 before his career began to spiral downhill. The Eagles even brought him back last year on the practice squad for a bit, and despite all the high-leverage mistakes I found that he had a great attitude when I chatted with him after he got here in September: "Yeah, the last couple years have just been very stressful for me," Watkins said at his locker. "But at the same time, I'm still pushing, still going and trying to get back to where I was. I definitely have a lot of football left. It's just a matter of when. When my time comes, when my time comes back around, I'll be ready to ball again." The 26-yarder Thursday night was his longest catch since a 30-yard touchdown from Jalen Hurts in a win over the Packers at the Linc in November 2022. Four years ago. Easy to dislike a guy like Quez Watkins, and I don't blame anyone who does. Me, I can't help admiring his determination and resilience. He's a survivor, and in the NFL that's not an easy thing to be.

7. Joe Milton had a pretty funny answer when asked about the nickname Bazooka Joe, which cleverly combines his first name with a metaphor for his massive arm. "I don't like it," he said. Why not? "Because bazookas only travel so far." What does he prefer? "Mr. Rocket."  You might remember that Randall Cunningham told the beat guys after the 1995 playoff loss in Dallas – his last game as an Eagle – that he nearly named his newborn son "Johnny Rocket." Randall put four names into a hat including "Randall Jr." and "Johnny Rocket," and his wife Felicity – fortunately – picked Randall Jr., born in Las Vegas the day before that 30-11 loss, went on to become a two-time NCAA Division 1 high jump champ for USC. And Randall didn't play again for two years, until Brian Billick and the Vikings revived his career.

8. Sixteen of the 27 all-time field goals of 55 yards or longer against the Eagles – 59 percent – have been made since Nick Sirianni became head coach. Kickers have made more field goals of 55 yards against the Eagles this year (five) than the first 77 years of the team's existence (four). From 1933 through 2017, opposing kickers were 6-for-23 against the Eagles from 55 yards and out (26 percent). Since 2018, they're 21-for-28 (75 percent). Ten of the 27 55-yarders against the Eagles have come since opening day last year.

9. How wild is it that the Patriots' leading receiver who's a former Eagle isn't A.J. Brown but Mack Hollins, still going strong in his 10th NFL season. Hollins, the Eagles' 4th-round pick in 2017, missed three games this year but has 23 catches for 293 yards in six games compared to Brown's 11-for-128 in four games. Hollins, who won a Super Bowl ring with the Eagles as a rookie, has really had a remarkable career with six teams, catching 231 passes for 2,912 yards and 18 touchdowns after a very slow first few years. He's 33 now and has the 4th-most catches ever by a WR after his 32nd birthday drafted by the Eagles behind Hall of Famers Harold Carmichael and Tommy McDonald plus DeSean Jackson. Good ol' Mack Hollins. Still at it.

10. When Kyle McCord started for the Dolphins against the Colts Sunday, he became only the fifth quarterback drafted by the Eagles in the sixth round or later to start an NFL regular-season game. The first was Dick Jamieson, the Eagles' 25th-round pick out of Bradley in 1959. He started only one game in the NFL, for the New York Titans in 1960 and won it, 27-3 over the Bills at the Polo Grounds. Bob Berry – the Eagles' 11th-round pick out of Oregon in 1964 – started 52 games with the Vikings and Falcons from 1966 through 1974. Koy Detmer, a 7th-round pick in 1997 out of Colorado, went 3-5 in eight career starts with the Eagles, and Tanner McKee, a 6th-round pick out of Stanford in 2024, is now 1-4 in five career starts. The Eagles have only drafted 22 quarterbacks in franchise history who have started an NFL game. Of the 49 quarterbacks who've started a game for the Eagles, only 14 were drafted by the Eagles.

#2730. U.S. court orders to proceed with planned ammunition detonation in Iowa

A U.S. appeals court on Monday cleared the Army to proceed with a controlled detonation of nearly five tons of degraded explosives at an Iowa ammunition plant, overturning a bid by environmental groups to block an emergency federal permit for the blast.

The order clears the way for the Army's plan to destroy around 68 barrels of lead azide at the Iowa Army Ammunition Plant in Middletown, near the Iowa-Illinois border, on Wednesday.

A three-judge panel of the 8th U.S. Circuit Court of Appeals denied a request by environmental groups seeking to challenge an emergency permit from the U.S. Environmental Protection Agency authorizing the operation.

"We are mindful of the very real risks presented by the Army's planned detonation of the stores of degraded lead azide," the ruling said. "However, we must acknowledge the realities of the situation: the degraded explosives present a risk of an uncontrolled and unplanned explosion, which would occur without the benefit of the safety measures that the Permit puts in place."

The planned blast, originally scheduled for Nov. 20, has been delayed three times amid regional concerns over possible health and environmental effects and criticism that the government failed to adequately communicate the potential risks to local communities.

The detonation is expected to produce a dust plume containing lead particles, a toxic metal that can cause serious health effects, according to the Iowa Department of Health and Human Services. Breathing in lead-containing particles or coming into contact with lead dust deposited on surrounding surfaces could pose potential health risks, the agency said.

The U.S. Army has argued that the risk of the material detonating on its own has increased as temperatures fall, and that the explosive compound, stored for more than two decades, has degraded to the point that moving it is too dangerous.

President Donald Trump said in a social media post Monday that he had told the Army to hold off until the court weighed in. He added that the decision would be subject to Iowa's Democratic Gov.-elect Rob Sand, who takes office in January, warning that further delay could risk "a large, spontaneous explosion."

In an X post late Monday following the court ruling, Sand said the Trump administration "should make this decision based on the information they have from the people they brought in to do the job months ago and have been on the ground in Middletown for weeks."

"Everyday Iowans are entitled to know how Middletown came to this situation, and learn why the administration believes this is the best path forward," Sand said, urging the federal government to share information with the public as soon as possible.

#2728. Anthropic was supposed to save the AI trade. What went wrong?

Anthropic's historic IPO — the largest in U.S. history — delivered a big win for the company and its investors. But it failed at another task: reviving animal spirits in the AI trade.

The Nasdaq Composite closed down 2.25% amid a broader market pullback with the S&P 500 declining 2% to 7,175.

Investors had been looking forward to the Anthropic IPO as a "clearing event" — reviving the tech sector after months of choppy trading, the Nvidia espionage scandal, and growing concerns that AI chip sales to Asia are likely to slump as Washington cracks down on potential violations of export controls.

Some of those worries resurfaced Thursday as a short seller, Hunterbrook Media, alleged in a report that firmware sold by chipmaker AMD had turned up in U.S.-blacklisted companies. AMD stock closed down more than 7%. The chipmaker told CNBC it complies with U.S. law.

Adding to the worries, the House Foreign Affairs Committee released documents showing that a subagency of the Commerce Department approved the sale of more than 879,000 AI chips to Chinese companies. That also raised the prospect of an Asia sales slowdown under stricter export controls.

Many investors and analysts continue to express confidence in the AI trade, noting that Anthropic's successful market debut — the stock closed up 17% from its IPO price and raised more than $81 billion — bodes well for capital spending across tech.

But investors acknowledged that this is a high-tension moment and said it's time to be more strategic about the trade.

Dan Niles, founder of Niles Investment Management, described himself as a "circumspect bull," making the comparison to market conditions in 2022 when the big indices tanked in the back half of the year.

"You could have still made money that year. You just needed to have the right combination of longs and shorts," he said.

Part of the challenge, Niles said, has been a price war between the two cutting-edge frontier labs, Anthropic and OpenAI. That has posed a threat to the overall revenue and profit potential of AI and the capex going into it. Fierce competition on price is producing collective losses between the two top companies, he notes.

"The combined revenue growth for OpenAI plus Anthropic went from growing 75% sequentially in Q2 to 48% in Q3," he told CNBC. "The combined losses went from $7.9 billion to about $8.4 billion from Q2 to Q3."

While uncertainty is high now, some resolution may be on the horizon if a greater degree of commercial and regulatory clarity emerges over the next few months.

Trading experts also told CNBC that the tech sector's troubles Thursday were a more serious matter than Anthropic simply siphoning capital from other parts of the market.

"There's a reset of AI in general," said JJ Kinahan, senior vice president at Cboe Global Markets.

Investors for years have profited in tech by buying the dip, he said, but "there's been an attitude change that [you] can't buy things blindly and they'll all go up." Nvidia's ongoing declines have shaken confidence in the buy-the-dip strategy, he said.

Anthropic's next few trading days will be a key gauge of the market's strength. Friday will be a "wild card," Kinahan said. It could be a "final flush out" for the market, or a bounce that leaves the market in "no man's land" while volatility remains elevated.

A better gauge may be Monday, when options on Anthropic start trading, providing insight into whether retail investors will back the company in force — providing more of a vote of confidence in the AI trade.

"If people think we're going up [on Anthropic] they'll buy stock, but retail particularly will just buy calls," he said. "There's a bigger test coming Monday."

For now, investors continue to sell AI-related stocks.

Neoclouds, the generally highly indebted AI-dedicated computing providers at the outer edges of the AI trade, were down substantially Thursday, with CoreWeave closing down more than 6%.

Hyperscalers such as Alphabet and Amazon.com, viewed as relatively defensive AI bets, also finished lower.

One bright spot: Cybersecurity stocks continued to rally, with Palo Alto Networks and CrowdStrike both closing up. AI is finding security holes across software infrastructure, creating a scramble to patch the vulnerabilities before hackers can exploit them.

— CNBC's Daren Fonda contributed reporting.
