#2703. Eagles vs. Steelers predictions for Week 11 of the 2026 NFL season

Eagles vs. Steelers predictions for Week 11 of the 2026 NFL season originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles (2-7) are back from their bye week and will host the Pittsburgh Steelers (6-3) on Sunday afternoon at the Linc.

Someday this year the Eagles will win another game. I don't think it will be this one. The Steelers do a lot of things well. They're third in the NFL in run defense and seventh in scoring defense, allowing only 20 points per game, and T.J. Watt is still terrorizing quarterbacks at 32 years old. Undrafted Jaylen Warren leads the NFL at 5.7 yards per carry and is a dangerous receiver out of the backfield, future 1st-ballot Hall of Famer Aaron Rodgers can still make plays when he's protected – he's got 15 TD passes and six INTs this year – and D.J. Metcalf isn't what he used to be with Seattle but still capable of coming up big a few times a game. The Steelres are 6-3 and 4-1 in their last five and they don't have a ton of quality wins, but they aren't playing a quality team Sunday afternoon at the Linc. The Steelers aren't a Super Bowl threat, but they do a lot of things well. The Eagles don't do anything well. Except lose. They're good at that. Getting DeVonta Smith back and a healthier Saquon Barkley should help Joe Milton, but I just don't see the Eagles competing for 60 minutes with a solid team like the Steelers.

I don't think this Steelers team is as good as their record but I do think they're better than the Eagles. Even though the bye week should definitely help the Eagles find some better answers on offense as Joe Milton learns the scheme, that Steelers defense is good up front. There are some serious concerns about pass pro holding up in this game and the Steelers are hard to run against. The one advantage the Eagles have is at receiver as DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks return. But Milton will need time to throw. The Eagles' defense should be able to slow down this version of Aaron Rodgers but I just don't have a lot of faith that the Eagles will be able to snap their losing streak.

Last week's bye week was a much-needed break for this Eagles team. Players needed to heal up physically and mentally. As a player, the bye week allowed me to decompress and refocus on the season. Even when the season is going well, getting away helps the mind and body reset for the remainder of the season.

Coaches don't usually get that break. They try to figure out how to get the players performing better, fix what's going wrong and reinforce what they should continue doing right.

On offense, getting DeVonta Smith back will surely boost the passing attack for Joe Milton. Helping the O-line with protection should be the offense's No. 1 focus. Markel Bell will need help against one of the best pass rushers in the league in T.J. Watt. They must send a chipping RB, TE presence and slide the line toward him. Sean Mannion cannot let the right side of the line wreck the entire offense.

Defensively, Vic Fangio will have to call an aggressive style of play. The DBs will have to play tighter coverage or Aaron Rodgers will pick the defense apart. Stopping the run without Jordan Davis will also be a priority. A collective effort will be required to compensate for losing Davis on 1st and 2nd down. Can the Eagles keep the win streak going against the Steelers who have not won in Philadelphia since 1965? Maybe. Will they? Probably not.

Fresh off the bye week with some reinforcements on the way at receiver, you'd have to hope the Eagles offense shows some level of improvement. Against the Giants, they managed just one score and 30 of the 60 yards gained on that drive came via penalty. It has been an abysmal effort of late. Having DeVonta Smith and more time on task for new starting quarterback Joe Milton should — in theory — lead to better results.

Aaron Rodgers turns 43 next month and just lost his top interior protector on the offensive line in Mason McCormick. That should spell a big game for Jalen Carter. He tends to feast on inferior opponents. However, the Eagles are down both of their defensive captains. Zack Baun is dealing with a back injury and Jordan Davis was just dealt to the Jaguars. I suspect they'll struggle to stop the Steelers' rushing attack, led by Jaylen Warren.

The Steelers haven't won a game in Philadelphia since 1965. That changes on Sunday.

The Steelers are much better on paper than the way they are actually playing. Pittsburgh has a great defensive line… that doesn't get a lot of pressure. Talented receivers… to go along with one of the worst passing offenses in the NFL. The stats don't match up to the talent you see on the Steelers team. However, they are good at two things — running the football and winning close games. Two things the Eagles cannot seem to figure out so far this season. I am encouraged to see if the Eagles offense can make any strides with Joe Milton's second game at quarterback and the return of DeVonta Smith and Wicks. The Eagles' defensive line has a winnable matchup against a banged up Steelers offensive line but Aaron Rodgers is known for getting the ball out quick. I don't trust anything the Eagles are doing right now to be able to win this game.

It's good to have football back in the city of Philadelphia. I know it's been a frustrating campaign to say the least, but when football is in town, the city has a different type of energy to it. Against the Steelers, you know exactly the type of ballgame it's going to be. They want to play a slow, monotonous brand of football where they run the ball, churn clock, smother you defensively, and leave town with a win without doing anything spectacular. They're a veteran-laden unit headlined by future Hall of Famers in Aaron Rodgers and T.J. Watt, but it's their blend of youth-infused talent that showcases a well structured football team.

For the Eagles, I'm excited to see Joe Milton once again with what looks to be a WR core at full strength at time of writing. With extra time to prepare via the bye, I expect Milton to make some throws this week allowing his guys to make a few plays downfield. Pittsburgh is underwhelming in the secondary, and if Milton is granted just a few ticks of time to work through his progressions, the Eagles could create some explosive plays this week. Everything, however, starts with the offensive line, and I hope this coaching staff gives Markel Bell help against Watt or the longtime Steeler could have a handful of sacks by himself this weekend. That simply cannot happen. Double teams, chips, sliding protection, do something to help out your rookie RT whose confidence looks to have wavered mightily since the Jacksonville and Carolina games.

Defensively, limiting RB Jaylen Warren is the key to this week. He's the most underrated player in football in my book and the explosive engine that gets this whole thing going on offense for Pittsburgh. Limit him, and you have more than just a shot at winning this ballgame. My eyes are also on the edge rushers to create pressure on the soon-to-be 43 year old Rodgers, and the LB core which could be without Zack Baun. Shedding blocks with consistency and tackling in open space has lacked within this defense — that must improve on Sunday. Overall, I haven't seen this team adjust in months and this Steelers team is a rugged ballclub that just got embarrassed on Sunday Night football against the Bengals. Do I hope HC Nick Sirianni and OC Sean Mannion made adjustments during the bye week? Absolutely, but, my confidence in executing said changes is a whole other conversation.

#2702. Will Richard receives a postgame flopping fine

The NBA has issued a $2,000 fine to Golden State Warriors guard Will Richard for violating the league's anti-flopping rule during Golden State's dramatic 88-87 home victory over the Boston Celtics on Sunday night.

Richard, who logged 17 minutes off the bench and finished with three points, two rebounds, and two assists in the one-point thriller, was assessed the penalty following the league office's postgame review. Under the NBA's crackdown on embellishment, non-unsportsmanlike flopping violations carry a standard $2,000 fine.

Despite the minor setback to his wallet, the second-year guard out of Florida remains a key rotational piece for head coach Steve Kerr. Playing through a torn ligament in his left thumb earlier this month, Richard has continued to provide energetic perimeter defense and floor spacing, averaging 7.3 points and 2.3 rebounds per game while helping Golden State improve to 9-8 on the season.

#2700. Brooklyn Nets’ defense falls flat in 142-116 loss vs. Timberwolves

There's only so much that can go right on one end of the floor when nearly everything is going wrong on the other. Great defense means little if you can't score, and excellent offense doesn't matter if the defense is weak.

Brooklyn Nets learned that lesson again on Monday night against the Minnesota Timberwolves, 142-116, dropping them to 3-13 and marking their eighth double-digit loss of the season. It was yet another season-high for Brooklyn's opponent and fifth straight loss.

It didn't take long for that imbalance to show.

By the end of the first quarter, Brooklyn trailed 41-28. In his first career NBA start, following Nolan Traoré's hamstring injury, rookie Mikel Brown Jr. scored eight points in eight first-quarter minutes, shooting 3-of-5 from the field and hitting both 3-point attempts.

Julius Randle also dominated his former team, posting 29 points in 35 minutes along with four rebounds and four assists.

Entering the night as the league's 16th-ranked offense, Minnesota had its highest-scoring quarter of the season to start things off on Monday, dropping 41 points. It wasn't a particularly sluggish quarter for the Nets offensively; they scored 28 and hit an impressive 5-of-6 from deep.

However, defense was the biggest issue, allowing the Timberwolves to hit seven threes and win the fast-break points battle 10-0.

The story remained much the same in the second quarter. While the Nets tied the quarter, with 33 points apiece, the same exact issues prevailed. By the end of the first half, both teams were shooting over 50% from beyond the arc. The Nets still lost the fast-break points battle by a large margin (18-2), committed 12 turnovers against Minnesota's eight, and allowed 18 points off those compared to their 10. They were also dominated inside, allowing 30 points in the paint compared to their own 18.

Regardless of how connected the team's offense is, it's difficult to win games with that level of struggle on the defensive end of the floor. From the perimeter to the paint to the fast break, Brooklyn's defense simply couldn't find a groove in the first half. Monday also marked the fourth time in the past five games that the team has allowed over 70 points in the first half.

Their best defensive outing in that stretch came Friday, when they surrendered 62 to the Orlando Magic.

After the third quarter, however, the Timberwolves took a 115-91 lead. The tale of the game continued. Minnesota also finished the third quarter by scoring on 18 of their final 20 possessions. That just isn't a recipe for winning games, even though Brooklyn shot 13-of-22 from 3-point range through three quarters.

It wasn't all ugly. There are certainly some individual performances to highlight.

In his first start, Brown Jr. impressed with 17 points in 29 minutes, coupled with five rebounds and seven assists. He led the team in first-half points but didn't score in the second half. The gripe was his turnovers, logging six against seven assists.

In his second straight start over Egor Dëmin, Danny Wolf also provided a meaningful all-around presence for Brooklyn. Wolf compiled 11 points, five boards, and four assists in 25 minutes, including a 3-of-5 mark from downtown.

At the end of the day, though, until the Nets find some semblance of defensive consistency, even their excellent offensive outings will be hard to turn into wins.

It hasn't been the easiest beginning for Mikel Brown Jr. After all, how could it be? He's been on on a minutes restriction to begin his career. He had himself a solid outing on Monday night in front of a national crowd.

#2699. As Anthropic shares tank, investors scramble to hedge. It could be very expensive

As Anthropic shares tumble toward their IPO price of $83, investors are looking for ways to hedge. Unfortunately, they may have to wait until Monday, when options on the AI giant are expected to list.

If Anthropic breaks its IPO price, it will represent a 27% decline from Thursday's intraday high of $113. The wild price swings offer clues on how expensive it might be to buy protection on the new tech giant once options begin trading.A 20% intraday move points to an implied volatility somewhere between 100 and 150 for Anthropic options, according to estimates from single-stock market-makers on the Cboe trading floor in Chicago.

Options traders are also looking at how SpaceX traded in the days following its offering for clues on how options might price.

Implied volatility in SpaceX started at 116, but that didn't deter options traders big and small from piling into the contracts tracking the stock, with about 1.7 million contracts traded on the first day for a total options premium of about $2.5 billion. Far out-of-the-money call contracts were some of the most popular trades as the stock soared 40% in its first three sessions.One key difference between now and then? Anthropic is trading against the backdrop of S&P 500 volatility close to 30, compared to 17.3 when SpaceX debuted. "I expect ANT options to be super active," said Tom Sosnoff, co-founder of the trading platforms ThinkOrSwim and TastyTrade. "I expect heavy options volume."One thing Anthropic and SpaceX have in common is heavy retail involvement in both IPOs, with retail brokerages receiving a 20% allocation from SpaceX and reports pointing to between a 15-20% allocation from Anthropic on Thursday. "Round one of the Anthropic IPO easily goes to the individual investor," Sosnoff added. "Retail participants bailed early at the best prices of the day while institutional investors paid up to top off their underwhelming allocations. I believe retail was aggressively reloading in the mid-90s."Further signs of retail demand will become evident when options likely begin trading on Monday. One thing to watch: the spread between out-of-the-money call and put contracts, which was heavily skewed toward calls in the case of SpaceX. "I would be careful comparing Anthropic to SpaceX," said Dennis Davitt, CIO and Co-Founder of Millbank Dartmoor Portsmouth. "From a market-structure perspective, the two are dramatically different. The availability and cost of borrowing will be different, the companies are at very different stages of maturity, and SpaceX has allowed employees to monetize portions of their holdings over time. All of those factors matter when pricing options."

#2696. Sixers Bell Ringer: Sixers undone by Portland’s blazing hot shooting

Sixers Bell Ringer Season Standings:VJ Edgecombe – 4Jaylen Brown – 3.5Tyrese Maxey – 3.5Joel Embiid – 3LeBron James – 1Anfernee Simons – 1Dean Wade – 1

The Philadelphia 76ers simply could not stop the red hot hands of the Portland Trail Blazers in a 128-111 loss on Monday night.

Joel Embiid did end up missing this one due to illness after spending the day as questionable on the injury report. Jabari Walker started in his absence. The Sixers were otherwise healthy.

This was a shootout early with some hot shooting from both teams in the first half. The Sixers hit 57.8% from the floor and 37.5% from long range, with the Trail Blazers hitting 50.0% and 46.3% respectively. It all culminated in a LeBron James buzzer-beating three to end the half and knot things at 69-69 going into the break.

The Trail Blazers, unfortunately, remained red hot from long range. It was a dual problem: partially due to the Sixers' help defense leaving way too much room for guys on the perimeter, and partially due to Portland just knocking down absolutely everything. They continued firing as the Sixers went cold, building a double-digit lead early in the final frame. Shooting regression certainly had hit Philadelphia… but Portland seemed immune. The Sixers couldn't hit a damn thing and the Trail Blazers were able to pull away fairly comfortably.

The Sixers are now 14-4 on the season as their win streak has been cut short at five games. They're off Tuesday and will be back on Wednesday evening for a Thanksgiving Eve game.

Until then, let's get to the Bell Ringer. Most of these candidates are here for their first half effort more so than the rough, cold second half that affected pretty much everyone in a Sixers uniform.

Lebron James: 23 points (8-for-13 field goal, 4-for-5 3PT shooting), 6 rebounds, 3 assists

(Photo by David Dow/NBAE via Getty Images)

Especially in the absence of Joel Embiid, there was extra work on LeBron James shoulders to really be the engine of the Sixers' offense. Of course he was up to the challenge, he's the King. After a first half of efficient shooting and some patented incredible dimes to teammates, LeBron had 16 points on 6-for-8 field goal, 3-for-3 three-point shooting with three assists going into the break. Those three triples included a buzzer beater to tie the game at the end of the second frame. He suffered from the same cold stagnant streak that hit the rest of the Sixers in the second half, but it was an incredible few periods from LeBron.

James finished with 23 points on 8-for-13 field goal, 4-for-5 three-point shooting with six rebounds and three assists.

Jaylen Brown: 20 points, 2 rebounds, 4 assists, 2 steals

(Photo by Jesse D. Garrabrant/NBAE via Getty Images)

Jaylen Brown just makes it look so easy sometimes. And he made it look easy on a night returning from a few games off due to a hamstring strain. Whether it was being in the right place at the right time or working isolation to create shot opportunities out of nothing, Brown looked healthy and ready to work tonight. By halftime, Brown was leading the Sixers with 19 points on 8-for-10 field goal, 1-for-2 three-point shooting with three assists. He unfortunately picked up his fourth foul early in the third which sent him to the bench and, to be honest, the Sixers' offensive momentum took a seat with him. That was the beginning of the end for the Sixers. Really illustrates how critical Brown can be to this team's offensive groove.

Brown finished with 20 points on 8-for-14 field goal shooting with two rebounds, four assists and two steals.

Tyrese Maxey: 22 points, 2 rebounds, 5 assists

(Photo by Jesse D. Garrabrant/NBAE via Getty Images) | NBAE via Getty Images

It was far from Tyrese Maxey's best night, I'll be honest. He had some inconsistencies shooting the ball so he couldn't really get going for any of the one-man streaks he tends to string together. But he wasn't discouraged, and instead kept waiting for better opportunities to come and chipped in points where he could get them to fall. This was especially true in the third period — critical, as everyone else for the Sixers went relatively cold during that frame — where he knocked in a a number of big buckets to keep the Trail Blazers close as they tried to pull away. After scoring just 12 in the first half, Maxey added 10 points in the third to give the Sixers a fighting chance. They didn't take advantage of that by any means, but they still had a chance.

It was an up and down night, but Maxey ended up one of the Sixers' best scorers — 22 points on the night with two rebounds and five assists.

#2694. Mr. Rocket finally hits his target to ice a much-needed win

Mr. Rocket finally hits his target to ice a much-needed win originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Joe Milton missed a bunch of throws on Sunday. It was far from a perfect game against the Steelers in his second-career NFL start.

But with the game on the line, Milton came through in the Eagles' 19-16 win.

With just 1:09 remaining, the Eagles were facing a 3rd-and-6 from their own 39-yard line. They could have run the ball again, forced the Steelers to use their final timeout, and punted the ball away. Instead, Nick Sirianni got aggressive and put the ball in Milton's hands.

Milton said it meant a lot that the coaching staff trusted him in that situation. He repaid that trust when he hit DeVonta Smith with a perfect pass for a seven-yard gain to ice a win that snapped a seven-game losing streak.

"I told [Smith] in the huddle that I'm going to come back (to him)," Milton said. "Just keep running your routes hard. Those small things that he's coming back this week, just small things that we have to clean up and get better at and get more dialed in. That just takes more practice and reps, just completing the ball to him and getting him a friendly football."

Before that completion, Milton had completed just 10 of 20 passes in the game as the Eagles' offense completely dried up in the second half. To make matters worse, Milton missed several throws that he really needs to make.

But there's something special about a guy who can keep his confidence after mistakes and can make a play like that after a less-than-stellar game. It's something Milton has struggled to do at times.

"Every play you go through out there, that's the only play that matters," Milton said. "Once that play is over with, you have to know how to flush it. Like I talked about previously, I've been through a lot of adversity. I've been in a lot of situations where you put me in that situation six years ago, and I probably would've still been thinking about the [previous] play and not being able to flush it. Being in the moment now, I'm being where my feet are, I'm able to flush the play and move on with the next one."

Milton is known for his strong right arm — his nickname is Mr. Rocket — and we saw that again on Sunday. Sometimes that was good, sometimes that was bad.

In the first half, Milton was able to scramble to his left before throwing a cross-body pass on a rope to Darius Cooper for a 29-yard gain on 3rd-and-13. But there were other plays where Milton couldn't harness his velocity.

On one play, he nearly threw a ball through Makai Lemon. And on the Eagles' first offensive play of the second half, he overthrew DeVonta Smith on what would have been a 68-yard touchdown.

"On throws like that, I usually say my daughter's name because it helps me relax," Milton said. "I didn't at that time, obviously. … I saw it, and I just got real excited because before that play I just told him we're gonna go home. It's time to go home. It's time to go score. When I've got the look that I wanted, I just got overly excited. But yeah, you just got to hit those, and especially in those moments."

Milton says he really does sometimes say his daughter's name — Maleia was born on Oct. 9, 2025 — before unleashing a throw. It helps him relax.

"Basically it kind of tells [me] in my mind that I'm throwing the ball to her, so it can't be as hard," Milton said. "Show a little love."

The Eagles are now 3-7 as they head to Dallas to face the Cowboys on Thanksgiving. The Eagles signed Milton off the Cowboys' practice squad on Oct. 16. A little over a month later, he's going to start against them.

The Cowboys scored 51 points on Sunday, so if the Eagles are going to win, they're probably going to need Milton to show off that arm again in a few days.

And if you needed any reminder that Milton has a strong arm, he was wearing a rocket pendant around his neck after his first NFL win as a starter. He bought that chain for himself when he went to the Combine a couple years ago.

"Yeah, that's me, Mr. Rocket," Milton said. "It's on my wall in my game room. That's me, that's my name. I love it. I'm blessed to be able to throw the ball far, and however strong my arm is. Yeah, Mr. Rocket, I love it. It's me."

In that scenario, the Stock Trader's Almanac thinks more churn is to come. "The low is more likely in mid- to late December than this week."

#2689. Terrion Arnold's lawyer seeks sanctions for discovery violation

Seahawks cornerback Terrion Arnold remains on paid leave with eight felony charges pending in Florida. And the case against him is moving forward.

On Monday, attorney Harvey Steinberg filed a motion for sanctions, arising from the alleged failure of the prosecution to timely disclose as discovery material.

The gist of the 13-page filing is that the prosecution withheld a statement from defendant Boakai Hilton until after another defendant, Arianna Del Valle, sat for a sworn, pre-trial deposition.

Hilton’s statement, per the filing, conflicts with statements made by Del Valle. By not having the Hilton statement, Steinberg was unable to properly question Del Valle about the discrepancies.

On the surface, it’s an easy fix. Steinberg should get another chance to question Del Valle under oath. At a deeper level, the situation (if it unfolded the way Steinberg alleges) speaks to the integrity of the process.

Prosecutors have immense power and discretion, which should be exercised at all times with the goal of doing justice. Sometimes, a blind desire to “win” can overcome the mandate to do justice, thereby corrupting the system.

The Hillsborough County prosecutor’s office is already dealing with an unrelated case in which “winning” may have trumped justice. A prosecutor recently resigned for failing to disclose during jury selection that the sister of a fellow prosecutor was in the jury pool for a rape trial. The woman ultimately served as the foreperson of the jury, which returned a conviction.

A new trial has been ordered, and the prosecutor who failed to disclose the relationship has resigned.

It all comes back to integrity. Are the prosecutors playing games in order get a conviction or a favorable plea deal, or are the prosecutors committed to justice in whatever form it may take?

If Steinberg’s contentions are accurate, it looks like someone may have been trying to sandbag Arnold’s defense in an effort to make it harder to draw helpful testimony out of a key witness. In a case like this, the cards should always be on the table — with no one trying to keep anything up their sleeve.

#2688. Saquon Barkley healthier after bye, hoping to salvage his season

Saquon Barkley healthier after bye, hoping to salvage his season originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Before the bye week, Saquon Barkley was battling through hamstring and back injuries for a few games.

As the Eagles get ready for the second half of their season, Barkley is definitely feeling healthier. But it doesn't sound like he's 100 percent, nor will he be.

"I wish the bye week was like a magic pill and everything just vanishes," Barkley said. "But that's only in la-la land, I guess. But it definitely did help."

Barkley, 29, has had a disappointing season. Injuries have limited him, but he has just 69 rushing attempts for 296 yards and 3 touchdowns. It's obviously not all his fault, of course. Barkley is playing behind a struggling offensive line in a dysfunctional offense.

But he also doesn't think he is personally playing to his capabilities.

"I think the biggest thing you could do in a bye week is go back and watch yourself on film," Barkley said. "Watch other football, watch other games and see how well other backs are doing. And just be honest with yourself. If I'm being honest with myself, the last couple weeks, for whatever reason, I don't like the way I was playing and what I was putting out on the field.

"The way I go about changing that is the only way I know how to, just put my head down and go to work, just continue to be consistent. But it's got to look different. It's got to look different than what it was in '24 and '25 because every season is going to be different challenges. Felt really good this week to be able to have a full week, go out there and practice and put the work in. And hopefully I see the reward on Sunday."

The last two games for Barkley haven't gone well. Against the Commanders, he had 13 carries for 30 yards and against the Giants, he had 13 carries for 22 yards. His yards-per-carry average dropped from 5.6 to 4.3 on the season after those two losses.

So Barkley and his teammates happily welcomed a bye week on the heels of a seven-game losing streak.

"I think it was helpful for everyone," Barkley said. "From staff to everyone that works in this building to players. Sometimes when a season is not going the way you wanted it to go and you're facing a lot of adversity, that week off is really important. More so for just the mind also. Obviously, the body. It's good for the body. But just the mind and getting your mind somewhere else a little bit. And then regroup and come back ready to work."

As the Eagles look toward the last eight games of the season, they are in the basement of the NFC East with a 2-7 record. They are not technically dead just yet, but it would take a mini miracle for them to pull themselves out of this.

But that's easy to say on the outside. For the guys in the locker room, they have to keep fighting even after they eventually get eliminated. That starts with Sunday against a Steelers team that ranks third in the NFL in rushing defense.

"One game at a time," Barkley said. "I said it already, I'm going to say it again. We put ourselves in this situation. Only way we're going to get out of it is by digging ourselves out. I still believe we have the men and women in this locker room and in this building to get it done. … Technically, we're still in the division and obviously has that crossed my mind? Of course. But can't even take it there.

"Just have to be focused on one game at at time and staying present and taking advantage of the opportunities and this opportunity we have coming up against a really good team who's trying to get back on track and definitely didn't like the way that they played on national television."

#2687. Skip Bayless takes subtle shot at Micah Parsons with a Patrick Mahomes reality check

Micah Parsons is expected to make his long-awaited return from injury this week but Skip Bayless seems to have questioned why it took him so long.

Parsons injured his left knee in Week 15 of the 2025 season and it is understood he could now make his comeback on Wednesday when the Green Bay Packers clash with the Los Angeles Rams.

After that news emerged, Bayless took to X to make a comparison between the Packers star and Patrick Mahomes, who also injured his knee around the same time last year but returned to the field at the beginning of the 2026 season.

He wrote: "Patrick Mahomes and Micah Parsons both injured their knees last December 14 and needed surgery. Mahomes returned for this season's opener September 14 and has played all 10 games. Parsons is 'expected to play' for the first time this Wednesday night November 25."

Although it was not mentioned in Bayless' tweet, Parsons also tore his meniscus. That injury, combined with his ACL tear, typically takes anywhere between 8-12 months to recover from, so Parsons is still technically on track to recover as expected.

That is just one argument fans have made when hitting back at Bayless' comments on the Packers' ace, with one writing: "Parsons and Mahomes didn't have the same injury. While both tore their ACL, Micah also tore his Meniscus. So he had surgery for the ACL and then the other one in June. Reality is – his knee was more messed up than Mahomes' was."

A second commented: "How ignorant. Parsons had an additional meniscus tear."

Several were also quick to point out that Parsons had his surgery later than Mahomes and unlike the Kansas City Chiefs star, he had to have another one in June: "Parsons also had his surgery 2 weeks later than Mahomes and had another one in June."

The size of the defensive end compared to quarterback Mahomes and the fact that they play different positions was also brought up in response to Bayless, with a fan saying: "Parsons had a more significant tear, is 20 pounds heavier, and as a recall, had a setback in the summertime. Two different issues."

"Parsons is a DE he needs to make sure that knee is strong enough to get off. QB is a little easier coming back from that," added another.

A study also found that, on average, NFL players need 11.5 months to recover from ACL surgery, which is longer than soccer stars due to the high-impact nature of their sport. In turn, with defensive end likely a more demanding position than quarterback on the physical front and additional surgery required, Parsons' slower return time probably isn't that surprising in reality.

#2685. Future Eagles Fits: Five interior defensive linemen to watch in 2027 NFL draft

Future Eagles Fits: Five interior defensive linemen to watch in 2027 NFL draft originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Welcome back to Future Eagles Fits, our weekly deep dive into the prospects who could eventually find their way to Philadelphia.

Every week, we're going inside the tape and looking at prospects through an Eagles-centric lens — breaking down the athletic profile, positional versatility and scheme fit that could ultimately determine whether a player makes sense in Philly. Along the way, we'll examine how prospects are being evaluated across the league as draft boards begin to take shape and conversations are had with league representatives across the scouting landscape.

For the Eagles, the interior of their defensive line has quickly become a position to monitor as the organization begins to look ahead. A first and second round pick in return for Jordan Davis is outstanding additional draft capital to work with, but it also leaves a significant piece of the defensive front to replace moving forward. Jalen Carter remains the centerpiece of the group, while Moro Ojomo, Ty Robinson, and Byron Young will have opportunities to take on larger roles within the rotation. Still, adding another young, explosive presence to the interior could go a long way toward maintaining the physicality and disruptive ability that has become such an important part of the Eagles' defensive identity.

The good news? It's an intriguing class up front with several players who offer unique skill sets that could make sense for what the Eagles are looking to add. Whether it's finding another high-end interior pass rusher, adding a versatile defensive lineman who can align up and down the front, or bringing in a bigger-bodied presence who can control the line of scrimmage, there are legitimate options to consider.

While there is still a ton of football left to be played and the entirety of the pre-draft process still to play out — which will alter boards — here are five interior defensive linemen to know in the upcoming class — two potential Day 1 targets, two Day 2 prospects and one Day 3 option who could provide some additional juice up front.

Day 1: Xavier Gilliam, Tennessee | 6'2″, 307 lbs (verified measurements)

If you're looking for an interior presence who can immediately change the way an opposing offense has to approach its protection schemes, it starts with Gilliam in this class.

A former transfer from Penn State, Gilliam has developed into one of the most explosive defensive players in the entire country this season and he's currently a top-10 player on my personal board. Below is a quick peek at the type of pop he provides up front.

At nearly 310 pounds, Gilliam has an outstanding combination of lower-body explosiveness, strength and initial quickness off the snap. He doesn't need much space to generate momentum, and his ability to explode out of his stance allows him to create immediate problems for guards attempting to establish angles.

There's a level of violence to his game that really stands out, as well. He plays with outstanding energy, consistently attacks the line of scrimmage and has the type of first-step quickness that can make even well-positioned offensive linemen uncomfortable. He's not simply a defensive tackle who occupies space and looks to disrupt the run game, he has legitimate pass-rush juice and the athletic profile to create negative plays without needing a blitz or additional help from the second level.

For Philly, he and Carter would be a true nightmare to counter due to their explosiveness up front. Add in Ojomo – we'll see if they do in fact extend him – and all three would give the Eagles an outstanding foundation along the line of scrimmage. If you're looking to invest first-round capital in an interior defensive lineman, finding someone who can make an impact in both phases of the game is a pretty good place to start. He's one of the most exciting defensive prospects in the entire class.

Day 1: Devan Thompkins, Alabama | 6'5″, 290 lbs (verified measurements)

Gilliam is the explosive interior disruptor, but Thompkins might be the most versatile defensive lineman in the entire draft.

A transfer from USC to Alabama, Thompkins has the opportunity to be the first interior/edge hybrid selected next April. At 290 pounds, he brings a unique physical profile to the position, and I'm not sure there's another player in this class who offers quite the same flexibility when it comes to moving around a defensive front.

The biggest selling point with Thompkins is that he doesn't necessarily need to be confined to one particular alignment. He can line up directly across from a guard and attack the interior, work outside an offensive tackle, or even operate as a wide-nine defender outside the tight end. It's an incredibly valuable skill set for any defensive coordinator who wants to manufacture different looks, create favorable matchups and keep opposing offensive lines guessing.

There's real power behind his game, too, which matters for young players making the transition to the pro game. It can't all be finesse, and Thompkins has herculean strength to generate movement at the point of attack, and his ability to attack from different alignments makes him particularly difficult to counter from down to down. He has the athleticism to challenge offensive tackles on the outside, but also possesses the strength and power to create problems when working inside against guards.

What makes his evaluation particularly interesting is the number of different ways an NFL coaching staff could utilize him. Want to kick him inside and create a mismatch against a guard? Sure. Line him up outside the tackle and let him use his length and athleticism to get after the quarterback? That's another option. He's the type of player who gives a defensive coordinator the freedom to be creative with personnel and alignment without sacrificing physicality, and for the Eagles, that versatility could be especially appealing. Philadelphia has traditionally valued defensive linemen who can move around the front and create problems from multiple alignments. With Carter and Ojomo already providing interior options, Thompkins could give the Eagles another player who allows them to change their defensive presentation depending on the opponent.

Day 2: A.J. Holmes Jr, Texas Tech | 6'2″, 300 lbs, 32 ⅜ arm (verified measurements)

Holmes is a name whose draft stock has continued to trend upward throughout the process, and there's a lot to like about what he brings to the interior defensive line.

At 300 pounds, he has a compact, powerful frame that allows him to hold his ground against offensive linemen while also providing pass-rush upside.

He's capable of winning against individual blocks with his power using his upper-body strength to create separation and disrupt the timing of opposing run concepts, but he's not a player who needs to rely exclusively on athleticism to make an impact. There's a physical element to his game that allows him to compete at the point of attack and create problems for offensive linemen who aren't prepared for his initial punch.

He's also shown the ability to provide something as a pass rusher, which is particularly important when evaluating interior defensive linemen in this range. Holmes has enough quickness to attack gaps and create pressure from the inside, giving him a pathway to contribute on passing downs rather than simply being a rotational run defender. His ability to generate disruption without requiring a favorable matchup is an important part of his evaluation, and he's excelled so far this year totaling 11 sacks through 10 games for the Red Raiders.

There are still areas to develop, particularly when it comes to consistently anchoring against combination blocks and maintaining his positioning when offenses devote additional attention to him, but that's part of the appeal of finding a player here on Day 2.

For Philly, Holmes would provide another young body who can compete for snaps in the middle of the defensive line. Established pieces are present, but continuing to add depth and competition to the rotation is important, particularly after moving Davis. Holmes has the ability to contribute against the run while offering pass-rush upside, making him an intriguing developmental option who could eventually grow into a larger role.

Day 2: Jordan Hall, Georgia | 6'4″, 320 lbs, 35 ¼ arm (verified measurements)

The Bulldogs have consistently produced NFL-caliber defensive linemen, and Hall is another player whose combination of size, length, and physicality makes him an intriguing prospect in the class.

The bottom line for the Georgia product – he's an immovable force in the ground game.

There's a level of natural strength to his game that makes it extremely difficult for offensive linemen to create movement against him. He has the length to control a rep early on, size to occupy space, the lower-body strength to anchor against double teams and the physicality to make life difficult for opposing offensive lines attempting to establish their running game. He's not necessarily going to win every snap by making a spectacular play in the backfield, but his ability to screw his cleats into the grass and prevent offenses from creating movement at the point of attack is what makes him so appealing.

And sometimes, that's exactly what a defense needs.

There's also a natural fit when considering the Eagles' recent investment in Georgia defenders. Roseman has consistently shown an appreciation for the program's defensive talent, and Hall would give the Eagles another opportunity to bring in a player from a pipeline they know well.

After moving Davis, adding another big-bodied presence who can control the middle of the defensive line would make plenty of sense. Carter and Ojomo offer different skill sets, and Hall could complement those players by providing a more traditional interior run defender who can eat up space and make it difficult for opposing offenses to establish their identity on the ground.

Day 3: Jide Abasiri, USC | 6'4″, 281 lbs (verified measurements)

I hope Abasiri ends up at an All-Star game in the pre-draft process for another evaluation exposure as he could end up being one of the more intriguing value selections in the middle to late stages of the draft.

A young prospect who just turned 21 years old, Abasiri has all the physical tools NFL teams drool over when going through the evaluation process. At 6-foot-4 and 285 pounds, he brings excellent length to the interior defensive line and has shown enough flashes throughout the season to suggest there's a lot more potential to unlock as he continues developing.

The first thing that stands out is his physical profile, as Abasiri has the type of length that can create immediate problems for opposing offensive linemen, particularly when he's able to extend and establish his hands to keep blockers from getting into his frame. For a younger player, that combination of size and length is an appealing starting point, especially when considering how much development can take place within ideal coaching surroundings in the NFL.

At this stage of the process, consistency is going to be an important part of his evaluation. There's a difference between flashing the ability to win at the college level and consistently executing those techniques against NFL-caliber offensive linemen. Abasiri still has room to develop his overall game, particularly when it comes to translating those physical tools into more consistent production, and it's a main reason why he's in this range to begin with.

Right now, he's someone who could land anywhere from the late third round into the early-mid portion of Day 3, depending on how the rest of the evaluation process unfolds. His age, physical makeup and flashes of disruption give him an appealing developmental profile, and a strong finish to the season followed by a good showing throughout the pre-draft process will absolutely help his stock continue to climb.

For the Eagles, they don't necessarily need every defensive lineman it drafts to come in and immediately command a significant snap share. Finding younger players with legitimate physical upside who can develop behind established veterans is an important part of maintaining a productive defensive front over time, and Abasiri has the length, athleticism and developmental runway to make him an intriguing option later in the draft.

#2684. Cavs lose 122-108 to Bulls after walloping in first half

The Cleveland Cavaliers lost 122-108 to the Chicago Bulls after taking a beating in the first half. Donovan Mitchell led the team with 28 points.

#2683. Real Madrid vs PSV Eindhoven, 2026 Champions League: Predicted lineups

Real Madrid host PSV in what should be a manageable home game for Los Blancos in the Champions League, where they remain unbeaten. José Mourinho made some rotations against Celta Vigo, so key starters like Valverde, Tchouameni or Cucurella will almost certainly return to the starting lineup today.

Real Madrid predicted XI: Courtois, Valverde, Rudiger, Huijsen, Cucurella, Tchouameni, Bernardo Silva, Güler, Bellingham, Vinicius, Mbappé

PSV Eindhoven predicted XI: Kovar, Dest, Geertruida, Obispo, Kostic, Sano, Mijnans, Perisic, Til, Van Bommel, Pepi

#2682. Colts-Texans draws tepid 12.23 million on Thursday night

NFL viewership is driven largely by quality of matchup. The Thursday night matchup that launched Week 11 may have driven some viewers to examine their life choices.

Still, 12.23 million watched the game between the Colts and Texans, on average. It was the smallest Thursday night audience of the season, and it represented a nine-percent drop from last year’s Week 11 Thursday night game between the Jets and Patriots.

The game also faced competition from the release of the new Grand Theft Auto video game.

The latest game dragged the full-season average under 17 million for the first time this year. It now stands at 16.62 million per game.

The Prime Video schedule concludes with Broncos at Steelers on Black Friday, Chiefs at Rams on December 3, Vikings at Patriots on December 10, 49ers at Chargers on December 17, Texans at Eagles on December 24, and Ravens at Bengals on December 31.

Flexing is an option for some of those contests. The window opens in Week 13, with the move “generally” coming no later than 21 days before the date of the game.

At this point, Vikings-Patriots is only 17 days away. Flexing remains possible for the final three games — with 49ers-Chargers possibly on the brink of being moved. The Week 15 Sunday schedule otherwise includes Bengals-Panthers, Browns-Giants, and Cowboys-Rams.

#2681. Finally a glimpse of what the Eagles' defense is supposed to look like

Finally a glimpse of what the Eagles' defense is supposed to look like originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

This is what it's supposed to look like. Terrific against the run, relentless pressuring the quarterback, numbers to the ball, tight pass coverage, sure tackling.

It's a brand of Eagles defense we just haven't seen enough of this year.

On another day when the offense had a tough time scoring points, the defense carried the team to its first win in two months, 19-16, over the Steelers at the Linc. The Eagles improved to 3-7, ending their seven-game losing streak.

"I thought we played well," Cooper DeJean said. "We didn't need to give up the late touchdown. But we put together a solid game. And now we've got to keep stacking. We're going to continue to do this. We're going to fix things that we need to fix."

The Eagles held the Steelers to 255 yards – 60 below their season average – and 3.7 yards per carry – a full yard per carry below their season average. Aaron Rodgers threw for 227 yards but needed 44 passes to get there. That's the fewest yards he's ever thrown for in 32 career games where he's thrown at least 44 passes.

After allowing 27 points and 392 yards per game during the first six games of the seven-game losing streak, the defense has put together back-to-back solid performances, allowing 19 points and 249 points per game in a loss to the Giants and 19 and 255 in their first win in two months.

After recording just 10 sacks in their first eight games, they have 11 in the last two. And after allowing 132 rushing yards per game through eight games, they've now allowed just 77 per game the last two.

Every way you measure it, they've started to figure things out.

Sunday at the Linc, they were at their best, shutting down a Hall of Fame quarterback and the NFL's rushing average leader.

"We just came to play," said Jonathan Greenard, who had one of the Eagles' five sacks. "Stopped the run early. Got to make them get out of what they were comfortable being in. And we just executed when we needed to.

"Sometimes, man, you need to have a little bad side or something negative or a little bit of adversity to hit a little bit to kind of show you that, hey, man, just because you wear this Eagles uniform, they're not just lay down solely because of the history or the past success you have.

"I mean, this is an innovative league, so (if) you're not on that steady progressive upward trend, you get passed up. So I think that it just kind of took a little bit of that, sadly. And it happens around the league sometimes. I've been on teams where it happened like that. And I think we just responded well."

Considering the Eagles just traded away their best run stopper, the Steelers' lack of success running the ball was especially notable.

Jaylen Warren came into the day leading the NFL with 5.7 yards per carry. He ran nine times for just 28 yards with a long gainer of 11 yards.

"It helps out the whole game when you can stop the run and make a team pass," Jeremiah Trotter said. "Then you've got your guys that can rush and get sacks and put pressure on the QB. It really just makes the game look a whole lot easier when you're going to stop the run."

Maybe the biggest story of this game was how the defensive front without Jordan Davis controlled the line of scrimmage, shutting down the run and pressuring Rodgers.

Byron Young, Gabe Davis and Ty Robinson all played more than they have been and Young picked up two sacks and Davis one and all three played a part in stuffing Pittsburgh's vaunted running game. Young came in with only 2 ½ career sacks and nearly doubled that and Davis, a practice squad call-up, had the first of his career.

"It was amazing," Moro Ojomo said. "You know, at the end of the day, it was sad to lose J.D. But some other guys got an opportunity, you saw it today with Byron, you know, Byron's hungry, Ty's hungry, right? Even Gabe. So it was amazing to see those guys succeed."

There have been some ugly Sunday afternoons for this defense. The Bears put up 27 points and 375 yards. The Rams 24 and 415. It was 25 and 399 for the Panthers, 38 and 416 for the Cowboys, 27 and 339 for the Commanders.

But this is a proud group, and the last two weeks and especially Sunday against the Steelers they've held their own and showed that there's still some juice on this defense.

The Cowboys are up next Thursday in Arlington, Texas, and considering they're averaging 37 points and 421 yards in their last five games, this won't be easy. But maybe the Eagles have discovered something finally on that side of the ball.

"I mean if you have a sense of pride you got a little bit of pride, you ain't just gonna sit there and take a beating you know what I mean?" Ojomo said.

"What type of man are you? What do you represent? Who are the people that you're setting an example for? Your family members, your kids, whatever it may be. You know what I mean? You want to be able to look those people in the eyes and know that you did your best."

#2680. Mikel Brown Jr. impresses in first start during Nets night to forget against Timberwolves

MINNEAPOLIS — Nolan Traore's hamstring injury is a tough break for the Nets and their second-year pro. But a golden opportunity for rookie Mikel Brown Jr.

With Traore sidelined for at least 8-to-10 weeks, Brown finally got his first NBA start Monday night in Minnesota. It may not have been a winning one for the lottery pick, but it was an impressive one.

The Nets got drubbed 142-116 by the Timberwolves before 18,329 at Target Center. But Brown's debut in the lineup was one of the few bright spots.

Brooklyn Nets guard Mikel Brown Jr. (0) drives past Minnesota Timberwolves center Rudy Gobert (27) during a game in Minneapolis, Monday, Nov. 23, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

"I felt great," Brown told The Post. "Obviously that first wind is different from when you're coming on the bench, so I got a little gassed for a bit in the first half. But once I found my rhythm, I was flowing. But it was great to be out there.

"In the first half we played well. But defensively, we just can't give up 140 points. We let them catch their rhythm early and that's what happens when you give good teams rhythm early on in the game. They had their rhythm throughout the game. We've got to cut that water off early. Take away those wide-open shots, those wide-open dunks that they had early on in the game and we would've been fine."

Traore was diagnosed with a right hamstring strain, opening the door for Brown, who had heretofore been on a minutes limit and treated with kid gloves.

But Monday the Nets (3-13) finally took the training wheels off. Brown finished with 17 points, a career-high seven assists and five rebounds, albeit six turnovers for a Nets team that looked fluid but far too cavalier and careless.

The Nets gave away 21 turnovers for 31 points. Combined with allowing 58 percent shooting and 17-of-39 from deep.

"It's disappointing," said Julius Randle, who had 29 points in his return to Minnesota but couldn't even pinpoint the single biggest defensive issue.

Grant Nelson (16) of the Brooklyn Nets drives to the basket against the Minnesota Timberwolves at Target Center in Minneapolis on Monday, Nov. 23, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

"We've got a lot. We were better earlier in the year and now we've got some slippage in multiple areas. So we've gotta figure out where it is. But defense just starts with competitive nature first and then go from there."

Brown was a rare silver lining. He poured in all of his points in the first half, then had five of his seven dimes and all of his boards in the second.

With Brown at the helm, the Nets shot 52.6 percent and hit 18-of-36 from deep in his first game replacing the injured Traore in the starting lineup.

Brooklyn Nets guard Mikel Brown Jr. (0) dribbles against Minnesota Timberwolves guard Ayo Dosunmu (13) during a game in Minneapolis, Monday, Nov. 23, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

"It's unfortunate for the kid because he's had a great summer," coach Jordi Fernández said of Traore. "He's about the right things. He's worked really hard. He's improved his body, his game. You just saw his confidence out there and his personality and how he communicated [with] his teammates. Everything has been great. Now, it's not the end of the world. Obviously it's an injury that we feel for him; he's already started working and he'll be back at some point.

"Life goes on and we want him around as soon as he can be with the team. That's important because his energy was so good, his voice. So, 'next man' mentality."

That next man will be Brown for the foreseeable future.

Moritz Wagner of the Brooklyn Nets reacts toward a fan during a game against the Minnesota Timberwolves in Minneapolis on Monday, Nov. 23, 2026. Getty Images

Egor Dëmin not only didn't get the nod at point guard, he didn't start at all. He came off the bench for a second straight game, finishing with 10 points on 3-for-13 shooting and clear struggles.

"Yes, he can do better with this ball pressure and physicality, that's for sure, communication," Fernández said.

Jaden McDaniels had 34 for the Timberwolves (13-5).

The Nets fell behind 25-12 with 3:54 left in the first on a Jonathan Kuminga layup.

Brooklyn gave up 10 unanswered to turn a six-point deficit into a 16-point hole. They trailed 74-61 going into the break, and couldn't rally.

At 86-77, the Nets coughed up a 20-3 run that blew it open altogether.

"A lot of things we could have done better, starting with our physicality, ball pressure, contesting shots," Fernández said. "We had hands down from the beginning of the game. That's happened already too many times."

#2679. Bears have gone five straight games without ever trailing for first time since 1985

The Bears’ win over the Saints on Sunday was their fifth consecutive game taking a first-quarter lead and winning without ever trailing. That gives this Bears team an accomplishment the franchise hasn’t achieved in 41 years.

The last Bears team to win five straight games without ever trailing was the 1985 Bears, one of the most dominant teams in NFL history.

That 1985 Bears team lost just one game, a Monday night classic against the Dolphins in the 13th game of the season, and after that loss to the Dolphins they never trailed in the final three games of the regular season, or in the two playoff games they won to earn a trip to Super Bowl XX. In the Super Bowl, the Bears fell behind 3-0 in the first quarter before winning 46-10, snapping their streak of five straight games without trailing.

This Bears team isn’t dominating quite like that 1985 team, but the Bears are currently on a very impressive run: They haven’t just won five games in a row, but they’ve scored in every quarter of all five games, the franchise’s first streak of 20 consecutive quarters scoring since the 1941 Bears. That team, like the 1985 team, won the NFL championship.

The 2026 Bears are playing like some of the best teams in franchise history. They’re jumping out to early leads and never looking back.

#2678. The Nasdaq is in freefall. Here's one stock to look at

I asked Claude after Friday's close what I should do about the tanking stock market. Its advice was surprisingly useful: avoid panic selling and look for opportunities. A 40 VIX, which it touched Friday, might provide those opportunities. Specifically, a higher VIX (higher options premiums) signals fear, but it also creates an opportunity to sell elevated premium in some premium stocks, like Amazon.

Amazon's cloud business is growing at its fastest pace in nearly five years. AWS revenue rose 39.3% YoY in the third quarter, and management reported a $578 billion backlog. Trailing 12-month operating cash flow rose 29% to $168.3 billion. However, infrastructure investment accelerated, resulting in more than $42 billion in negative cash flow. The competing forces of accelerating growth and the cost of supplying it could keep the stock in its range over the next four weeks. Its shares are only off 14% from its recent highs, comparatively better than the Nasdaq Composite's near 19% decline.

AWS demand supports the lower end of the range. The backlog gives Amazon a basis for adding capacity even as investors scrutinize the cash cost. Cash spending could temper an upside rerating.

The next leg higher may require evidence that new capacity can earn attractive returns at this scale, and that evidence may be in short supply over the next couple of months.

Sell the Dec. 18, 2026 AMZN 235/285 strangle for an indicative $5.90 credit per share.

Max reward — $590 per strangle if AMZN finishes between $235 and $285 at expiration, before fees.

Breakevens at expiration — $229.10 and $290.90.

Max risk — Unlimited on the uncovered call, but one should think of the trade as risking getting short at a net price above $290, a nearly 13% premium to today's closing price. The downside loss would reach $22,910 per strangle if AMZN fell to zero. The credit is roughly 2.3% of a $260 reference share price over four weeks, or nearly 30% on a simple annualized basis. That comparison uses the share price as its denominator. Actual return on capital depends on margin and the cost to close or settle the trade.

Buying in bear markets, as the Nasdaq just touched, is never easy. But those opportunities don't present themselves too often. What are you going to do with it?

#2677. Can returning Beattie and Haughey make an impact?

Michael McArdle has decided the upcoming friendly double-header against Greece is the perfect time to bring back Kerry Beattie and Ella Haughey from the international wilderness.

Glentoran midfielder Chloe McCarron is also in under McArdle for the first time after a lengthily absence, but the Glentoran midfielder would likely have been included sooner if not for a spell on the sidelines through injury.

Beattie won her last cap a year ago while Haughey will hope to make a senior debut in one of the two games against Greece at Inver Park after she was an unused substitute in friendlies against England and Scotland in her sole call up in 2021.

The inclusion of both players, who have been in good form at their clubs, shows McArdle is willing to keep the door open for a return to the international squad if players are performing at club level.

Magill returns to NI squad for Greece friendlies

Portsmouth striker Beattie has been in and out of squads since her debut in 2021.

The 24-year-old has four goals in 13 caps, the last of which she won against Iceland in the Nations League play-off a year ago.

A prolific forward during two spells at Glentoran, she has struggled for consistency in moves across to Scotland with the likes of Glasgow City, Hearts and Aberdeen.

She left the Dons in the summer and consulted with McArdle when deciding upon her next move, before joining WSL 2 side Portsmouth.

Beattie has eight goals in all competitions for a Pompey side languishing at the bottom of the table and McArdle is pleased she has settled in well.

"Kerry had spells at Hearts and Aberdeen and for whatever reason it didn't quite work out at both, she had good spells but not a consistent run to express herself," McArdle said.

"The move to Portsmouth, I was delighted she reached out to me for some advice, it's an alignment for her and it's been a great move on and off the pitch. It's allowed her to enjoy herself but also play 90 minutes and be that focal point striker.

"She will have been disappointed not to make the squad for the [World Cup] play-off [against Portugal] with her form but she worked hard and she's back in, I'm delighted for her."

Beattie faces stiff competition for the starting striker role from the likes of returning captain Simone Magill and Kess Elmore, who impressed in the last camp and scored against Portugal, but she should receive some minutes across the two games and will be keen to grasp her chance to make an impact.

Left-back Haughey was called up to the senior squad as a 16-year-old under Kenny Shiels, but did not feature [Getty Images]

West Brom defender Haughey's last involvement with NI came when she represented the under-19s in 2023.

A Lisburn Rangers player when Kenny Shiels included her in the senior squad as a 16-year-old, Haughey then moved to Nottingham Forest before settling at the Baggies, who play in the National League.

The left-back, 22, has been a regular this season for the club in the third tier and combines playing with her university studies.

Left-back has been a problem position for NI which McArdle has been trying to address as demonstrated by him bringing in Lucy-Jayne Grant in the last camp and Haughey for this window to allow Rebecca Holloway to play as a central defender.

Haughey will be competing with Grant to fill that full-back role on the left side and McArdle believes she is more than capable of doing so having tried to bring her back into the squad earlier in his reign.

"Ella's been at West Brom for a few years now. She has real potential, strong, athletic potential. We tried to call her up sooner but there were some challenges," he explained.

"She's probably been one of their top performers and one of the top performers in the league. Delighted she's earned this opportunity and hopefully she can make her debut and achieve her first cap."

McArdle has made a concerted to continue to widen the player pool available to him as he aims to build depth ahead of the Nations League campaign next year, but if Beattie and Haughey catch the eye it will show that giving chances to those previously involved under different managers could be another possibility.

#2676. Eagles-Steelers matchups to watch in Week 11

Eagles-Steelers matchups to watch in Week 11 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles (2-7) are hosting the Pittsburgh Steelers (6-3) at the Linc on Sunday afternoon at 1.

The biggest move Howie Roseman made before the trade deadline was sending Jordan Davis to the Jacksonville Jaguars for a first-rounder and a second-rounder. So this will be the Eagles' first game without their run-stuffing nose tackle and they're facing a team that is averaging 4.4 yards per carry on the ground — the fourth-highest average in the NFL.

The Steelers are 14th in overall rushing but have been efficient this season. Their ground game starts with running back Jaylen Warren, who has 126 carries for 719 yards and 2 touchdowns this season. That 5.7 yards-per-carry average is the best in the NFL among qualified players. Of Warren's 719 yards this season, 533 of them have come after contact. He's averaging 4.2 yards after contact per attempt, which is the fourth-highest average in the NFL. So it'll be important for the Eagles to wrap up in this game.

The good news for the Eagles is that they had their best run defense game of the season before the bye week against the Giants, allowing just 80 yards on the ground. It was the first time all season they held their opponent under 100 rushing yards.

Eagles LBs and S vs. Pat Freiermuth, Darnell Washington

The Steelers' leading receiver this season is DK Metcalf, who has 432 yards and 2 touchdowns on 35 catches. He is also leading the team in targets with 70. So maybe this is another good week to have Quinyon Mitchell travel with a top receiver. But the Steelers also have two tight ends who each see a ton of action in Pat Freiermuth and Darnell Washington.

Freiermuth: 38 targets, 27 catches, 270 yards, 5 TDs

Washington: 31 targets, 20 catches, 259 yards, 3 TDs

The Steelers have 15 passing touchdowns this season and 8 of them come from these two players. So the Eagles have to be aware of them in the red zone.

The Eagles have actually been very good against tight ends this season. They have allowed 38 catches for 386 yards and 3 touchdowns to opposing tight ends this season. Just two teams (Titans and Jaguars) have allowed fewer yards to opposing tight ends. But this will be a big test for their safeties and linebackers, especially if Zack Baun (back) is unable to play.

Aaron Rodgers is one of the greatest QBs in NFL history but he's obviously past his prime. Rodgers will turn 43 on Dec. 2, but he's having an OK season. He has completed 59.9% of his passes for 2,014 yards with 15 touchdowns and 6 interceptions. But he has already been sacked 26 times in nine games. The only QBs who have been sacked more this year are Deshaun Watson (37), Justin Herbert (33), Geno Smith (29) and Bryce Young (29).

Despite that, the Steelers are allowing a pressure rate of just 31.4%, which is the seventh-best QB pressure rate in the NFL. The 49ers are tops at 27.9%. While Rodgers is getting sacked quite a bit, he's also getting the ball out quickly. His average time to throw is 2.75 seconds, which is eighth in the NFL.

The Steelers are going to be without starting left guard Mason McCormick, who suffered an ankle injury in the loss to the Bengals.

The Eagles' defense finally broke out before the bye week with six sacks against the Giants after having just 10 entering the game. But with 16 sacks, they still rank 28th in the NFL this season and their pressure rate of 32.6% ranks 26th.

The plan this season was to have third-round pick Markel Bell sit and learn behind all-time great Lane Johnson but that plan got thrown into a blender earlier this year when Johnson abruptly retired. While Bell has had some rough moments, it'll be his job for the rest of the season. That became abundantly clear when the Eagles released Fred Johnson late last week. In six games, Bell has given up 5 sacks and 19 pressures. There have been ups and downs as the Miami product adjusts to the NFL.

This won't be an easy week. T.J. Watt leads the Steelers with 7 1/2 sacks and is second in pressures on the team with 23. (Don't sleep on Alex Highsmith or Nick Herbig; this is a dangerous group of edge rushers.) Watt, 32, is a future Hall of Famer and a former Defensive Player of the Year who is fourth on the active sack list with 122.5. He's behind just Von Miller (139.5), Cameron Jordan (132) and Myles Garrett (128.5). Oh yeah, and he plays most of his snaps on the defensive left, which sets up this matchup against Bell. The Eagles will need to help the rookie in this game.

Before the bye week, Saquon Barkley was dealing with hamstring and back injuries that were limiting him. Barkley looked good in limited action against the Cowboys but the last two games had 26 carries for 52 yards. Barkley has looked good when healthy this season but he's also running behind an offensive line that has really struggled this season. He'll see a tough test in Week 11.

The Steelers this season have the NFL's No. 3-ranked run defense. They have given up just 83.2 yards per game on the ground in 2026 and have held opponents to 43.3 rushing yards per game over their last three games. They have given up just 0.71 yards before contact per attempt.

DeVonta Smith vs. Asante Samuel Jr., Jamel Dean

In the Steelers' Week 10 loss to the Bengals, they had no answer for Jamar Chase, who ended up with 11 catches on 14 targets for 231 yards. Based PFF's data, Chase caught a pass against six different Steelers defenders but did most of his damage against their top two corners:

Chase vs. Samuel Jr.: 4 targets, 2 caches, 96 yards

Chase vs. Dean: 5 targets, 4 catches, 53 yards

The Steelers have been playing Jalen Ramsey at nickel corner and safety. He's no longer a full-time outside cornerback, so we won't see him on Smith in this game. At one point, the Steelers had a cornerback who would have had a better shot against Smith but they were unable to reach a contract agreement with Joey Porter Jr. and traded him to the Cowboys in late September.

Smith has missed the last three games with a hamstring injury but if he's healthy in this one, it could be a big opportunity for him in his first game with starting QB Joe Milton.

#2675. Player Grades: Cavs at Bulls – Poor effort results in a rough loss

The Cleveland Cavaliers fell behind by 22 points in the first half of this game as they blew an easy one against the Chicago Bulls.

Grades are based on our usual expectations for each player.

28 points, 3 rebounds, 5 assists, 2 blocks, 1 steal

Mitchell was largely absent for most of this game. His impact wasn't felt until the third and fourth quarters, when he grew more aggressive in trying to will his team back into the game. His scoring was too little, too late, as the Cavs lost this game when Chicago outscored them 45-20 in the second quarter.

Still, he takes our player of the game for getting to the line 12 times and converting all but one of those free throw attempts.

21 points, 6 rebounds, 5 assists, 7 turnovers

Harden got the Cavs back into this game through his downhill drives in the third quarter. He was as important as anyone in turning the tide after that ugly second quarter.

With that said, Harden shares just as much blame for their collapse in the first half. Turnovers and missed shots led to easy transition buckets for Chicago. And his half-court defense wasn't making up for anything.

Cleveland could have done a better job of finding Allen. He shot 6-for-7 from the floor but wasn't fed the ball enough for my liking. We know that Allen can quickly build momentum when he's being featured in the offense, and the Cavs never really tapped into that tonight.

Still, Allen failed to seize the one aspect of this that is within his control — offensive rebounding. Allen could have had more touches tonight if he had cleaned the offensive glass. Instead, he finishes with only 4 rebounds (1 offensive).

8 points, 10 rebounds, 3 assists, 6 blocks

The Cavs have something real in Watson's ability to block shots as a weakside defender. He's been a genuinely impactful defender in these scenarios. But there are more holes in the ship than just that, and so far, Watson hasn't been enough to stop the sinking.

Cleveland will need to get better support at the point-of-attack and in transition before getting the most out of Watson's defense.

Proctor is readjusting to his role now that Harden and Mitchell have taken back full control of the offense. He hasn't been as prominently featured as a creator, and that's led to quieter performances as of late. He's done well in this smaller role, but the Cavs should look for more ways to enable Proctor as this feels like they are currently selling him short.

Thomas looked like a stud tonight. His one-legged jumper from the baseline was the highlight, but for me, his inside finishes have been just as impressive. I wasn't expecting Thomas to be such a dynamic player at the rim this early into his career — but he's shown a level of touch and creativity that has won me over.

His grade takes a slight bump for being backdoor cut on defense throughout the first half.

Hezonja struggled with his shot and wasn't a great fit in any lineup tonight. He was on the wrong end of multiple defensive breakdowns, and didn't have the mobility to play the run-and-gun style that this game required.

12 points, 3 rebounds, 2 assists, 2 steals

Sam shot the ball well (3-for-6 overall) and was able to turn the corner against cheating defenders to get clean looks at the rim. He wasn't a difference maker on defense, but I don't think he was the biggest culprit, either.

The Cavs are still waiting for Tyson to figure things out. He's been in and out of the lineup recently, so that doesn't help. But if we're being honest, Tyson hasn't looked comfortable offensively for more than a game at a time since last season's All-Star break.

There's a tangible bump in intensity every time Omier is on the floor. He plays each game more like a sumo wrestler than a basketball player. His willingness to bury his defender beneath the rim on every possession is a big reason why.

Every other defender is trying to play defense, while Omier's man is trying to fend off a grizzly bear.

#2674. Bills list Greg Rousseau, Dalton Kincaid as limited on Monday's practice report

The Bills did not hold a practice on Monday, but they did issue an estimated injury report ahead of Thursday’s game against the Chiefs.

Linebacker Greg Rousseau (back) is on the report as a limited participant. Rousseau had a pair of sacks in Sunday’s win over the Dolphins and now has 10 sacks on the season.

#2673. 'We are in a new chapter' - Unai Emery urges Aston Villa to embrace hostile Istanbul crowd in tonight's Galatasaray Champions League test

Aston Villa boss Unai Emery has urged his players to embrace the hostile Istanbul atmosphere when they face Galatasaray in tonight's Champions League clash at Rams Park. Seeking to strengthen their push for automatic last-16 qualification, the Spaniard insists his side are building something different in a new European chapter.

Emery has called on Villa to show conviction and togetherness as they prepare for a demanding Champions League outing in Turkiye. A positive result against Galatasaray would underline Villa's steady European progress and edge them closer to the last 16. However, the manager knows individual quality alone will not be enough to handle the hosts' relentless intensity.

Emery is determined that his squad will not allow the intimidating surroundings or Galatasaray's high-energy approach to disrupt their rhythm. Having already beaten Turkish opposition heavily in his previous Champions League home ties, winning 6-1 against Bursaspor with Valencia in November 2010 and 6-0 against Fenerbahce on Matchday 2, the Spaniard recognises that an away trip to Istanbul demands total discipline.

Speaking to TNT Sports, Emery said: "We have to be focused on our game plan on the field. We have to help each other as well, always being positive with each other and try to feel strong, comfortable and confident playing, even when the crowd is against us.

"On the field, it's about playing football, winning duels, trying to build and keeping our structure as strong as possible."

Beyond the atmosphere, Emery expects a stern tactical examination from a well-drilled Galatasaray side packed with quality across every department. The Villa manager singled out midfielder Lucas Torreira for particular praise, while also acknowledging the strength of Okan Buruk's wider squad as Villa continue to evolve on the continental stage.

"Especially in the press, they have very good players," Emery said. "Torreira, in the middle, is really playing fantastic. They have very good wingers. They have very good centre-backs and very good goalkeepers. I think the coach [Okan Buruk] is also doing a very good job.

"But we are in a new chapter, and we are building something different now."

Villa head into Tuesday's clash at Rams Park needing just one more goal to match their entire 2024-25 league-phase tally of 13. However, breaking down Galatasaray on home soil will be no easy task for Emery's side. The Turkish hosts have won their last three home matches against English opposition, beating Tottenham 3-2 and Liverpool 1-0 twice.
